# Verification ledger

Every acceptance check from `docs/PLAN.md` Part C lives here with its real status.
Statuses: **VERIFIED** (machine, date, evidence) · **CI** (verified by the named CI
job) · **NEEDS-DEVICE(os/hw)** (written and `cargo check`-ed, runtime not yet
exercised — the user or a Claude Code session on that machine clears it) · **OPEN**.

Dev machine for this ledger unless stated otherwise: the build VM — Ubuntu 26.04 LTS,
GNOME on Wayland, VMware virtual GPU (no hardware encoder), 4 vCPU / 7 GiB,
Rust stable 1.96. No sudo: FFmpeg / PipeWire / ALSA dev headers are unavailable
locally, so those backends are compiled and tested by the Linux CI job instead.

| Check | Status | Evidence / notes |
|---|---|---|
| Host publishes onion service with persistent key; restart ⇒ same ID | VERIFIED the build VM 2026-08-28 | `tornect host` printed `Tornect ID: 5fgkeslwnfhhhlb24c7ghn5u3rigo3vlyhm6weqbd23qtr44chvoxzad.onion` 2 ms after client creation (before bootstrap), `REACHABLE` 73 s later (cold); after `SIGINT` and restart on the same `TORNECT_HOME` it printed the **same** ID (warm bootstrap 1.1 s, `REACHABLE` after 67 s); `tornect id --json` on that home gives the same `tornect_id` with `created:false`. Key lives in Tornect's keystore and is installed into Arti's (`keystore/hss/tornect-5fgkeslw/ks_hs_id.ed25519_expanded_private`); unit test `arti_derives_the_same_onion_address_as_tornect_core`. **Independently reproduced 2026-08-29 02:46–03:02 UTC** on fresh `target/tornect-test/v1`: `Tornect ID: l6tnrcetlwnqif75bbbovemd35lo73uadq47qwmgp2ivajmgd2aiauad.onion` printed 2 ms after client creation (cold bootstrap 19.2 s); `REACHABLE` only **325 s** after launch, and as `DegradedReachable` (one HSDir upload timed out); SIGINT → clean exit, state flushed 253 ms later (`guards.json`/`circuit_timeouts.json` mtimes; the `Flushed persistent state at exit.` log line is filtered by the default `tor=warn`); restart on the same home printed the **same** ID (warm bootstrap 0.95 s) and served 5 pings although Arti's status never left `Bootstrapping` in its 5 min 47 s of life (`REACHABLE` is Arti's upload self-assessment, not a prerequisite for connections); `tornect id --json` → same ID, `created:false`. Full logs: `docs/notes/m1-track1-evidence.md` (section "Independent verification (remediation pass)") |
| `tornect ping` over live Tor (client connects by onion address only) | VERIFIED the build VM 2026-08-28 | Fresh second instance (`TORNECT_HOME=target/tornect-test/h2`): cold bootstrap 8.7 s, rendezvous 9.0 s, `PING OK n=5 min/avg/max=737.2/813.3/972.4 ms`; warm re-run against the restarted host: bootstrap 1.7 s, connect 7.7 s, `PING OK n=5 min/avg/max=540.6/586.1/656.5 ms`. Host logged `answered ping #1`…`#5` both times. Final tree (run 8): connect 9.6 s, `PING OK n=3 min/avg/max=591.6/609.7/633.8 ms`, host `client disconnected after 3 ping(s)`, Ctrl-C ⇒ `Flushed persistent state at exit.`, exit 0, no stray output. Errors map to host-offline / host-not-running / timeout messages (`SignalError`); one real Tor transient (rendezvous circuit failure) was hit in 11 connects and reported as "Tor did not answer in time … retry". **Independently reproduced 2026-08-29** (fresh `target/tornect-test/v2` against the `v1` host above): cold bootstrap 8.7 s, connect **47.7 s**, `PING OK n=5 min/avg/max=844.6/947.3/1101.3 ms` (844.6 / 940.4 / 976.6 / 1101.3 / 873.5); against the restarted host, attempts 1 and 2 timed out at 60 s (`Tor did not answer in time…`, exit 1; host logged `hspool: Too many preemptive onion service circuits failed` and two HSDir upload timeouts) and attempt 3 connected in 5.7 s: `PING OK n=5 min/avg/max=539.5/560.7/617.1 ms` (550.1 / 539.5 / 549.9 / 617.1 / 546.7); host logged `answered ping #1`…`#5` and `client disconnected after 5 ping(s)` both times, exit 0. So on a bad Tor evening 2 of 4 connects fail and need the retry the message asks for; the "up to 2 min" / "≈ 70 s" reachability figures are typical, not bounds. Evidence: `docs/notes/m1-track1-evidence.md` |

| F1 fuzz: envelope + every body + both FSMs fed arbitrary frames, no panic (`cargo +nightly fuzz run envelope`) | VERIFIED the build VM 2026-08-29 (10-min run on the review-round harness) | `fuzz/fuzz_targets/envelope.rs` is a thin wrapper around `tornect_signal::fsm::harness` (decision M1-32): eight scenarios per input byte — host / client FSM × paired / unpaired × raw / re-signed — with a peer model that absorbs the FSM's in-window frames into the transcript and re-signs `HELLO` / `SESSION_REQUEST` (host role) or `HELLO_ACK` / `SESSION_ACCEPT` (client role) over it, ephemeral trust stores (nothing on disk), paused time; a panic in the FSM task is re-raised. What it reaches is proven by `fsm::golden::fuzz_seeds_match_the_golden_vectors_and_reach_a_session`: every committed seed (`fuzz/seeds/envelope/<scenario>.bin`, built from the golden vectors) drives its scenario through PAIR (unpaired) / consent / `SESSION_ACCEPT` / `PING`-`PONG` / `BYE` to an established session. Run: `cargo +nightly fuzz run envelope target/fuzz-corpus/envelope fuzz/seeds/envelope fuzz/corpus/envelope -- -max_total_time=600` with `rustc 1.100.0-nightly (e457a7b0d 2026-08-27)`, ASan, two consecutive 10-min runs on the same corpus (the second on the final tree, seeds only): `Done 717887 runs in 601 second(s)` then `Done 618535 runs in 601 second(s)`, ≈ 1.0–1.2k exec/s (CPU shared with a workspace build), cov 7526 → 7736 / ft 18641 → 19463 (the earlier 60 s smoke reached cov 4867 and never passed the second frame), corpus 1896 inputs, 0 crashes / 0 artifacts, exit 0 (`target/tornect-test/fuzz-envelope.log`, `fuzz-envelope2.log`). `cargo clippy --manifest-path fuzz/Cargo.toml --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean on stable (`xtask lint-all` does not cover `fuzz/`; adding it costs a second build of the Arti graph). **Review round 2026-08-29:** the libFuzzer scratch corpus that earlier runs had left under `fuzz/corpus/envelope` (1 576 files, untracked) was minimized on the final tree with `cargo +nightly fuzz cmin envelope` (`MERGE-INNER: 1584 total files` incl. the 8 seeds ⇒ `1317 new files with 8337 new features added; 1779 new coverage edges`, `target/tornect-test/fuzz-envelope-cmin.log`) and is now the committed corpus: 1 317 inputs, 13 995 bytes; the eight test-checked seeds stay under `fuzz/seeds/envelope/` (decision M1-36 records the policy: seeds committed and test-checked, `fuzz/corpus/<target>` committed only as a `cmin` result, scratch under `target/fuzz-corpus/`). Smoke run on the final review-round tree (client `PING` loop, `HostAudit`, session-popup budget, wire-domain checks included): `cargo +nightly fuzz run envelope target/fuzz-corpus/envelope fuzz/seeds/envelope fuzz/corpus/envelope -- -max_total_time=120` ⇒ `Done 102935 runs in 121 second(s)`, cov 7909 → 7934 / ft 19984 → 20080, ≈ 850 exec/s, 0 crashes / 0 artifacts, exit 0 (`target/tornect-test/fuzz-envelope-review-smoke.log`); the 10-min campaign on this exact tree is CI's / the next soak's |

| **A18** the pairing SAS resists a proxying MITM: neither side can choose its SAS contribution after seeing the peer's, and no unsigned/discarded `PAIR_REQUEST` byte reaches the code (§B.3, §B.6, decision M1-37) | VERIFIED the build VM 2026-09-04 | Found by the round-3 review and reproduced against the real HKDF construction before the fix: with the r6 `th_after(PAIR_REQUEST)` input, ~1500 candidates per side collide on the 6-digit code with ~90 % probability (hit at candidate 323; both screens `034 057`) for ~1500 Ed25519 signatures — inside the 20 s `HELLO_ACK` timeout. Now `HELLO` carries `sas_commit` (signed) and `PAIR_REQUEST` reveals `sas_nonce`; the host checks the commitment before any popup. `fsm::tests::pair_request_filler_cannot_move_the_sas` (32 bytes of `client_auth_pk` filler, host RNG scripted so `th_after(HELLO_ACK)` is fixed: the code is unchanged — and this test **fails** against the old derivation, verified by mutation) and `fsm::tests::pair_request_with_an_unopened_sas_commitment_is_refused` (wrong `sas_nonce` ⇒ `ERROR`, zero pairing popups) |
| **A19** a `keystore_locked` window cannot break the §B.8.13 log chain | VERIFIED the build VM 2026-09-04 | Found by the round-3 review: with `K_master` present but unreadable no tag can be computed, and the lines appended during that window landed untagged after the key epoch and past `log_chain_head`, so the startup verifier returned `Broken{"untagged line after the chain started"}` permanently once the recovery load restored the same key and epoch — a standing tamper badge with nothing tampered. Records are now buffered while `FailedClosed(KeystoreLocked)` and flushed **tagged** by the recovery load (capped at 256, overflow count written to the log). `unattended::tests::a6_log_chain_stays_valid_across_a_keystore_locked_window_and_its_recovery_load`; verified by reverting `store.rs` alone, which reproduces `Broken` at line 4 |
| **A14** (partial) unattended key material is wiped, not merely dropped | VERIFIED the build VM 2026-09-04 | Found by the track C review: `opaque-ke`'s `*FinishResult` types are `derive_where(Clone)` only — unlike the state types they are **not** `ZeroizeOnDrop` — so `export_key` (64 B, password-derived, stable across logins) and a second copy of the raw `session_key` survived every login, and `register_locally` left an `export_key` in the host process that owns the grant store. All four sites now wipe explicitly (`auth::wipe`). `auth::tests::key_material_is_wiped_not_just_dropped`. **Honest limit:** once `finish()` returns its frame is gone and no safe-Rust test can inspect it, so the test covers the wipe primitive and the observable contract (`ClientLoginOutput` carries no `export_key`); the call sites are covered by review. The full A14 core-dump sweep of §B.8.15 remains OPEN |
| **A7** a newer signed `SESSION_REQUEST` pre-empts a stale charged handshake (§B.8.4.5, `PREEMPTED` 0x002A) | VERIFIED the build VM 2026-09-04 for the cancel; the newer request getting in VERIFIED (unit) 2026-10-24 (audit B03) | Found by the track C review: cancellation is the rule and `UA_BUSY` the narrow case, but only the narrow case was implemented, so `PREEMPTED` was emitted nowhere in the workspace and a client whose circuit died was locked out for the full 30 s `AwaitKe3` timeout. The budget table now carries a per-pk cancel handle and a `charged` flag and the exchange is raced against it. `unattended::tests::a_newer_request_preempts_a_stale_charged_handshake` — fails cleanly in ~2 s against a build with the cancel disabled (verified by mutation). **Corrected 2026-10-24 (audit B03):** the test checked only the stale stream's `PREEMPTED`, never the newer request, and the newer request was refused `NOT_GRANTED` 0x0010: it reached `limiter.charge` before the cancelled handshake had settled its charge, so the client was still turned away (and told its access was never given). Fixed in `ab16c6c9` (see B03 under *Audit fixes*): the test now also asserts that the newer request is accepted, with a multi-thread variant |
| **B.8.5.2** host-wide budgets: the in-flight slot covers `AwaitKe3` only | VERIFIED the build VM 2026-09-04 | The slot was held for the whole handler, so four accepted sessions sitting in their popups and block windows could exhaust `MAX_IN_FLIGHT` and make a fifth granted device's login `UA_BUSY` with nothing in `AwaitKe3`. Released immediately after `settle`; `expire()` prunes entries older than twice the `AwaitKe3` timeout as a leak safety net. `unattended::tests::the_in_flight_slot_is_released_once_the_pake_is_settled` drives more successful sessions than `MAX_IN_FLIGHT`. The per-minute and host-wide caps themselves still have no dedicated test |

| PoW DoS defense + rate limits on | VERIFIED the build VM 2026-08-29 (configuration, admission policy, effort-ordered queue exercised live); OPEN: suggested-effort ramp under sustained load; OPEN: a cold Tornect client solving under a queue-filling flood (client-side follow-up, M1-4) | `tor-pow` feature default-on; service launched with `enable_pow=true`, `rate_limit_at_intro=(25/s, burst 200)`, `max_concurrent_streams_per_circuit=4`, `pow_rend_queue_depth=1024` (unit test `onion_service_config_builds_with_and_without_pow` reads every bound back from the built `OnionServiceConfig`); Arti wrote `hss/<nick>/pow_manager.json` + `pow_nonces/`. **Admission (third security review, high; decision M1-4)**: with PoW on the accept loop reserves a handshake *and* a circuit permit **before** polling Arti's request stream (`tor::Admission::Backpressure`, derived from the built config's `enable_pow`), so the overflow stays in Arti's effort-ordered, bounded (1024), expiring (300 s) queue — the only place Arti measures load and orders admission by proof-of-work; the second-review loop polled unconditionally and dropped the overflow itself, which kept that queue empty and the suggested effort at zero. With PoW off it drains and drops (`Admission::Drop`; Arti's PoW-off queue is an unbounded FIFO). Unit tests on the generic `supervise` loop with a poll-counting scripted stream: `backpressure_does_not_poll_the_request_stream_while_the_budget_is_full` (the stream is not polled while the one slot is held; the waiting request is served once it frees), `drop_policy_takes_every_request_and_drops_the_overflow`, `admission_follows_the_pow_setting_and_defaults_match_the_constants`. Live (`live_rendezvous_backpressure`, 2026-08-29 00:16 UTC, `target/tornect-test/review3-live2.log`): host published with a one-handshake / one-circuit budget (`TorNode::publish_with_limits`), three parallel `connect()`s from a second Tor node in the same process; the host accepted the three streams at 13.1 s, 21.3 s and 29.5 s (gaps 8.2 s and 8.3 s = 5 s hold + 2 s idle + handshake), every client got through on its first `connect()`, no request was dropped; one client's first introduction was refused by a stale intro point (`INTRODUCE_ACK: NOT_RECOGNIZED`) and Arti's retry carried a proof of work — `tor_hsclient::pow::v1: beginning solve, Effort(8) … solve complete … duration=11ms` — which the host verified and served: the effort-ordered path end to end — after a *fast* failure inside one Arti attempt series, the only path on which Tornect's client solves today (second OPEN item below). Host-side bounds after three security reviews (`tor::listener`, `ping`, unit-tested with scripted streams; decision M1-4): 32 rendezvous handshakes in flight (permit held only across `RendRequest::accept`, 30 s cap), 64 live rendezvous circuits, a circuit with no open signaling stream for 30 s is dropped (which closes it), a request that is not `BEGIN` on port 4000 destroys its circuit, a full accept queue refuses `BEGIN` with `END(DONE)` before accepting (10 s cap on `accept` and on `reject`), a stream write with no progress for 30 s fails with `TimedOut` on either side (`stall_timer_fails_a_write_with_no_progress_and_resets_on_progress`), and `ping::serve` disconnects a peer that sends nothing or takes no pong for 30 s (`stalled_pong_write_is_cut_after_the_deadline`), ends any session after 120 s or 100 pings (`chatty_client_is_cut_when_the_session_budget_is_spent`, `ping_budget_ends_the_session`) and reads at most 22-byte frames (`host_rejects_garbage`); `tornect host` serves at most 64 sessions at once. Residual exposure (M1-4): ≈ 1 dead-rendezvous-point introduction/s fills the handshake budget and ≈ 0.5 rendezvous/s the circuit budget — without PoW a lockout, with PoW an effort-ordered wait that a cold Tornect client cannot yet win (second OPEN item); per-device accounting is the envelope track's. **OPEN**: the suggested-effort *ramp* — Arti raises the published effort only for a 300 s period in which its queue was non-empty *and* at least `num_dequeued / busy_fraction` PoW-bearing requests arrived (`tor-hsservice-0.45.0/src/pow/v1.rs` 968–1036) — was not driven to a nonzero value here: the one-slot run lasted 35 s and `hss/<nick>/pow_manager.json` still holds `"suggested_effort": 0`. Recipe: `tornect host` with `TORNECT_LOG=info,tor_hsservice::pow=debug`; from another machine keep ≥ 40 `connect()` loops running against it for ≥ 10 min (more than the 32-handshake budget, so the host logs `rendezvous budget full; further requests wait in Tor's proof-of-work queue` — once per admitted request while saturated — and Arti's queue is non-empty for the update period; a Tor-side failure that surfaces *inside* one Arti connect attempt series makes the Arti client retry with effort ≥ 8 — a fast one such as `INTRODUCE_ACK: NOT_RECOGNIZED` does, a request that merely queues does not until Arti's rendezvous-completion timeout, ≈ 8 min on a cold estimator and so after `CONNECT_TIMEOUT`, and the effort resets on every `TorNode::connect()` (next OPEN item); only PoW-bearing arrivals count toward the ramp (`num_enqueued_gte_suggested`, 1150–1158), so run the loops from *warm* client state dirs — ≥ 100 recorded builds in `state/arti/state/circuit_timeouts.json`, so the rendezvous wait can end inside the 60 s and the retry solve — or the queue stays non-empty but PoW-free and the effort stays at 0); expect `suggested_effort` in `pow_manager.json` to become nonzero and new clients to log `beginning solve, Effort(N)` with N from the descriptor. A request whose client gave up while it queued still costs one circuit-build attempt when dequeued (`RendRequest` carries no receive time, `req.rs` 25–47), bounded by `RENDEZVOUS_TIMEOUT`. **OPEN (fourth security review, medium; decision M1-4)**: honest-client effort under a queue-filling flood. The third-review text said a client whose request went unanswered retries with effort ≥ 8; verified from the sources, that retry lives inside one Arti connect attempt series only (`HsPowClient` per series at the descriptor's suggested effort, `tor-hsclient-0.45.0/src/connect.rs` 929; no PoW state in the per-service `Data`, 78–125; a fresh isolation group per `TorNode::connect()`, `node.rs` 311, starts from a blank record, `state.rs` 561), and an *unanswered* request fails only by `RendezvousCompletionTimeout` after `rpt_ipt_timeout` = 3 × `BuildCircuit { length: 5 }` + 1 × `OneWay` estimates (`connect.rs` 1481–1518), which the Pareto estimator answers with the 60 s `cbtinitialtimeout` fallback scaled by length (≈ 3 × 150 s + 40 s ≈ 8 min) until it has `cbtmincircs` = 100 recorded builds (`tor-circmgr-0.45.0/src/timeouts/pareto.rs` 524–533, 617–638; the nine logged `tornect ping` runs left 55 in `target/tornect-test/h2/state/arti/state/circuit_timeouts.json`). `CONNECT_TIMEOUT` = 60 s bounds the whole connect (`node.rs` 314–336) and the CLI does not retry a `Timeout` (`tor_cmd.rs` 188, 297): a cold client's effort-0 request is the youngest of its class in Arti's queue, displaced by the next arrival once the 1024-deep queue is full (`pow/v1.rs` 1169–1177), and the client starts again at effort 0 on the next call. The low-rate flood of the third review *is* fixed (FIFO wait in Arti's queue, nothing dropped); the effort-ordered queue is not yet reachable by a cold Tornect client on the slow path. Not a listener change: the client-side follow-up (retries under a longer deadline inside `TorNode::connect` when the descriptor carries `pow-params`, or a Tornect-level effort floor across calls, which needs Arti to persist or accept a client effort) is tracked in M1-4 for the envelope track. Recipe (extends the one above): while the host logs `rendezvous budget full`, start one **cold** client — a fresh `TORNECT_HOME` (e.g. `target/tornect-test/cold`, no `state/arti/state/circuit_timeouts.json`) — with `TORNECT_LOG=info,tor_hsclient::pow=debug tornect ping <id>`; expect `tor_hsclient::pow::v1: beginning solve, Effort(N)` (N ≥ 8, or the descriptor's effort once the ramp is nonzero) within `CONNECT_TIMEOUT` (60 s) and a `connected in … s` line. As of this ledger it fails instead with `Tor did not answer in time` after 60 s without ever logging `beginning solve`; the item closes when that step passes |

| Arti state dir, keystore and onion service on Windows (`tornect host` reaches `REACHABLE` on a fresh `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Tornect` home) | NEEDS-DEVICE(windows) | Nothing about the Windows runtime is verified: the Arti graph (`ring`, bundled sqlite/zstd/xz, the HashX JIT via `dynasmrt`) is `cargo check`-ed for `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` here (M0 row above) and, since 2026-09-23, built and linked into `tornect.exe` for `x86_64-pc-windows-gnullvm` (the last section, *The Windows build of the whole app*), and the `windows` CI job runs `tornect-core`/`tornect` tests, not Tor. `fs-mistrust` checks **no** permissions or owners on Windows (`fs-mistrust-0.15.1/src/imp.rs` 138: `check_permissions` is Unix-only; README 218 "On Windows, we accept all file permissions and owners"), and `create_private_dir` (`tornect-core/src/keystore.rs` 104–112) sets modes on Unix only, so the state dir is protected exactly as well as `%LOCALAPPDATA%` (per-user profile ACL) and no more. Recipe: on a Windows 10 20H1+ machine, the cross-built `tornect.exe host` (or `cargo run -p tornect -- host` there) with no `TORNECT_HOME`; expect `Tornect ID:` then `REACHABLE` within ~2 min; from a second machine `tornect ping <id> --count 3` → `PING OK`; kill and restart the host → same ID; `icacls %LOCALAPPDATA%\Tornect\arti` must list only the user, `SYSTEM` and `Administrators` (if it lists `Users`/`Everyone`, add an explicit owner-only DACL in `create_private_dir` — `windows-sys` `SetNamedSecurityInfoW` — and record it in docs/DEPENDENCIES.md); if the host logs a HashX/`dynasmrt` JIT failure (PoW compiles a hash function into executable memory), `hashx` falls back to its interpreter by itself (`hashx-0.9.1/src/lib.rs` 76 `TryCompiler`), and `disable_pow_compilation = true` in `onion_service_config` is the explicit fallback — record which happened |
| Arti state dir, keystore and onion service on macOS (`tornect host` reaches `REACHABLE` on a fresh `~/Library/Application Support/Tornect` home) | NEEDS-DEVICE(macos) | Same status as the Windows row: `cargo check --target aarch64-apple-darwin` only (M0 row above), `macos` CI job without Tor. `fs-mistrust` applies its Unix checks on macOS, and `create_private_dir` sets `0700`, so a group/world-writable `~/Library` parent makes Arti refuse to start ("bad permissions"). The HashX JIT (`dynasmrt` → `memmap2` RWX mapping) is the platform risk: a hardened-runtime build without the `com.apple.security.cs.allow-jit` entitlement fails the mapping, which `hashx` handles by falling back to the interpreter (`hashx-0.9.1/src/lib.rs` 76) — slower puzzle verification, not a crash — and `disable_pow_compilation = true` is the explicit fallback (docs/research/arti.md §16.8). Recipe: on macOS 13+ (Apple silicon), `cargo run -p tornect -- host`; expect `Tornect ID:` then `REACHABLE` within ~2 min; from a second machine `tornect ping <id> --count 3` → `PING OK`; kill/restart → same ID; `ls -ld ~/Library/Application\ Support/Tornect/state/arti` → `drwx------`; run once more with `RUST_LOG=hashx=debug` while a client connects and record whether the compiler or the interpreter was used; for the notarized M8 build, decide entitlement vs `disable_pow_compilation` from that |
| `cargo deny check` (advisories, bans, licenses, sources) | VERIFIED the build VM 2026-08-28 | cargo-deny 0.20.2 (prebuilt musl binary under `target/tools/`, not installable via apt here): `advisories ok, bans ok, licenses ok, sources ok`; the two advisory ignores in `deny.toml` (RUSTSEC-2023-0071 `rsa` via Arti's ssh-key fork, RUSTSEC-2024-0436 `paste`) are the only ones needed; 36 `duplicate` warnings (`multiple-versions = "warn"`: RustCrypto 0.10/0.11 and rand 0.8/0.10 lines coexisting under Arti, docs/research/arti.md §3) and 6 unused license allowances; `bans.allow-wildcard-paths = true` added because the workspace's own version-less `path` dependencies were reported as wildcards. Output: `target/tornect-test/cargo-deny.log` |
| No OpenSSL / aws-lc in the dependency graph (`docs/research/arti.md` §2, `quic.md` §2) | VERIFIED the build VM 2026-08-28 | `cargo tree -i aws-lc-sys -p tornect-signal` and `cargo tree -i openssl-sys --workspace` both report `did not match any packages`; provider is `ring 0.17.14` via `rustls 0.23.43`. Now a gate: `cargo xtask verify` runs `cargo tree --workspace -i {aws-lc-rs, aws-lc-sys, openssl-sys}` and fails if any resolves; `install_crypto_provider` logs an already-installed foreign provider at `warn` with its name, and the unit test `install_is_idempotent_and_ring_is_the_provider_in_force` asserts the provider in force is `Ring` |

|---|---|---|
| Owner-only IPC endpoint: socket is 0600 and a foreign uid is dropped before any frame is read | VERIFIED the build VM 2026-09-05 | `tornect-platform::ipc` tests `the_socket_is_0600`, `a_request_round_trips_over_the_socket`, `a_stale_socket_is_replaced_but_a_live_one_is_not`, `an_undecodable_frame_is_answered`, `an_oversize_frame_is_refused`. Observed live: a running `tornect host` bound `srw------- … ipc.sock` and a second process reached it. The **foreign-uid rejection itself is not directly tested** — this machine has one user and no sudo; the check compares `UnixStream::peer_cred().uid()` against the uid owning the socket file this process created. |

Recipe: as another user, `socat - UNIX-CONNECT:<state_dir>/ipc.sock`; expect connection refused by the directory's 0700 mode, and — if the directory is opened up — the host to log `rejected an IPC peer owned by uid N` and close before reading a frame. |

Interactive path verified through a pty: a weak password refused twice with its reason, a passphrase accepted. |

Holds with no system bus too (the connection failure lands on the same variant), so it needs neither polkit nor a human. |

Recipe: `sudo install -m 0644 packaging/polkit/org.tornect.policy /usr/share/polkit-1/actions/ && sudo systemctl restart polkit`, then `TORNECT_TEST_POLKIT_ACTION=org.tornect.autostart cargo test --workspace -- --ignored --nocapture live_polkit_round_trip` (without the variable the test panics; `--nocapture` because it passes whatever polkit answers and prints the answer on stderr only: the check is that it prints `AUTHORIZED` after the right password is typed, and `DENIED` after Cancel; `UNAVAILABLE` means the policy file is not installed). The interactive path itself was proven during research against `com.ubuntu.UnattendedUpgrade.Pending.Postpone` (agent registered, dialog raised, blocking call, cancel, typed result). The console check of 2026-09-23 (second attempt) waited for the policy file from 22:55 to 23:38 (it was to be installed with sudo by the operator); it never appeared, so `live_polkit_round_trip` was not run: **NOT PERFORMED**. The third attempt (2026-09-24, 01:14–01:20) ended at its presence gate, before this part: **NOT PERFORMED** again. The fourth attempt (2026-09-24, 16:12–16:44, at `37a70e1`) passed its presence gate but ended at its first step (no grant), before this part: **NOT PERFORMED** a fourth time. The fifth attempt (2026-09-24, 19:42–19:50, at `996e6db`) got the grant and a pairing on the host, then was paused after the pairing, pending the operator, before this part: **NOT PERFORMED** a fifth time |

| Automatic fallback direct → obfs4 → Snowflake under real censorship | NEEDS-DEVICE(censored network) | Recipe: on a network that blocks Tor relays (or with the relay IPs firewalled), `TORNECT_PT_DIR=<dir with lyrebird> tornect tor-check`; expect `direct Tor did not work: no bootstrap progress for 40 s`, then obfs4, then Snowflake, and `state/tor-transport` = the rung that worked. Ladder order, stall/cap policy and last-good ordering are unit-tested (`tor::bridges::tests`) |
| Onion-service **hosting** over bridges | OPEN | code path supports it (bridge = first hop of every circuit); run `tornect --bridges snowflake host` + `ping` from a second instance |

Relative paths, group-writable binaries and the private-lines-replace-built-ins rule are unit-tested (`tor::bridges::tests`) |
| Windows: lyrebird.exe spawned by a console-subsystem tornect.exe shows no extra window | NEEDS-DEVICE(windows) | Recipe: `tornect.exe --bridges obfs4 tor-check` from a console; expect no new console window (research bridges.md §6) |

Every function the backend calls resolves against the VM's PipeWire 1.6.2 (`the_installed_library_has_every_function`) |

Capture worked: 3538×1919 at scale 1, first picture 0.21 s. The injector was refused as designed, with `Unavailable("the desktop did not allow remote control …")`. **Run 2**, with the switch on: `session started in 64.3 s … granted: true`; `first picture after 0.31 s, unprompted: 3538x1919`, so mutter sends a first picture of a still screen by itself; `pictures in 5 s with nothing moved: 23`; five pointer moves, each followed by a new picture; `pointer drag: 43 pictures in 2.3 s = 19.0 fps`; `LIVE PORTAL CHECK first_picture=true moves_with_a_picture=5/5 drag_pictures=43 token_kept=true`. The token file is 0600 |

## `serve_host` in `tornect host` (decision M1-42, 2026-09-21, fourth session)

Live rows: the build VM, real Tor, two `TORNECT_HOME`s (`target/tornect-test/s61-host`, `s61-client`),
both CLIs driven through pseudo-terminals. The driver answered a pairing `yes` only after
comparing the code each side printed. Passing run: 8/8 checks, 210 s. Evidence and the three
earlier runs: `docs/notes/m1-serve-host-evidence.md`.

| Check | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| The signaling port routes by the first frame: exactly `TORNECT-PING/1 ‖ nonce` → ping protocol, anything else → FSM | VERIFIED | `tornect_signal::dispatch::tests` (8). They cover: a replay across 3-byte reads; the ping protocol served over the replay; only the exact 22-byte magic frame counts as a ping; an over-cap header gets the FSM's own `E_TOO_LARGE`; garbage gets the FSM's `ERROR`; a peer leaving early gets nothing; one deadline covers the whole first frame (paused time); a full client handshake runs over the replay. Live: `tornect ping` against the wired host, `PING OK n=3 min/avg/max=566.2/628.1/705.5 ms` |
| `tornect connect` pairs with the same code on both screens, then runs an attended session | VERIFIED the build VM 2026-09-21 | Host `code: 919 121`, client `It must be exactly 919 121`. This was the M1-32 re-pair: the client ran with `--forget`, and the host asked about a device "ALREADY PAIRED … (refusing does not unpair it)". Then a control session (`a72997036d6c27cb`) with 3 session pings (642–1045 ms), and `SESSION ENDED (by you)` / `session ended: the client left (USER)` |
| A known device skips pairing; a view-only answer is honoured | VERIFIED (same run) | Second connect: no pairing question; `SESSION LIVE c7557e511c6b8d2d … (view-only)` on both sides |
| `tornect panic` ends a live session on both ends | VERIFIED (unit + live) | Unit: `host::tests::ipc_panic_ends_a_live_session`, which fails when the IPC context gets its own kill switch (`Timeout("session frame")`). Live: `Sessions ended. …`; host `session ended: ended here (PANIC)`; client `SESSION ENDED by the host (PANIC)` 0.6 s after the command |
| The guard sees live sessions: access-increasing changes are refused during a session and for 60 s after | VERIFIED (unit + live) | Unit: the same test (`On` → `G_REMOTE_SESSION_ACTIVE` during the session and in the cool-down). With the guard told that no session runs, `On` went on to the polkit dialog instead. Live: `tornect unattended on` during the session → `a remote session is live or ended less than 60 s ago (0x0101)` |
| One tracker drives the FSM's `BUSY` answers and the guard's view | VERIFIED | `fsm::tests::the_live_session_tracker_drives_busy_and_the_guard_view`: a second device's pairing is `BUSY` while a session runs, and gets its popup once the session ends |
| A host `BYE` between pings is reported as the host's, not as a lost connection | VERIFIED (unit + live) | Found by live run 3: after a panic, the client reported `SESSION ENDED (connection lost)` / `Circuit closed`, because it read only at its next ping and Tor had closed the circuit first. Fixed: the client now waits on the stream between pings. `tor_cmd::tests::a_host_bye_between_pings_is_reported_as_the_hosts` fails on the old loop with `Lost(Frame(Io(Kind(BrokenPipe))))` |
| Consent is answered only on a terminal; input typed before a question never answers it | VERIFIED for every question after a process's first; **the first question is OPEN** (SLICE-B2 B2-3: the stdin reader starts after the drain, so typed-ahead lines answer it; a claim, not yet reproduced) | `prompt::tests` (5). `tornect host </dev/null` prints the refusal warning. In live run 2 the driver typed an answer before its question appeared: the prompt discarded it, and the popup ended `POPUP_TIMEOUT` |
| Ctrl-C ends every session with `BYE` and exits cleanly | VERIFIED (live) | `Ctrl-C: shutting down`, exit 0 |
| The same flow between a Linux and a Windows machine | NEEDS-DEVICE(linux + windows) | `tornect.exe` is built (the last section, *The Windows build of the whole app*). Recipe: on the host, run `tornect host` in a terminal and note the `Tornect ID`. On the other machine: `tornect ping <id> --count 3` (Tor reachability without pairing), then `tornect connect <id>`. Compare the code on both screens and answer `yes` on each; the host answers `a` or `v`; `tornect panic` on the host ends the session |

## Session layer and media over Tor (decisions M1-43, M1-44; 2026-09-21, fifth session)

| Check | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| A media stream is served only after a valid `Bind`: the fixed-size first frame arrives within 10 s, reading it takes no slot, at most 8 `Bind`s for sessions not registered yet wait (one more is refused at once), a bind is single-use, a wrong MAC neither binds nor burns the session, and a bind that beats `on_start` waits for it | VERIFIED | `tornect_session::tests::{unbound_streams_end_at_the_bind_deadline, waiting_binds_beyond_the_cap_are_refused_at_once, a_bind_is_single_use, a_wrong_mac_neither_binds_nor_locks_the_viewer_out, a_bind_that_beats_the_registration_waits_for_it, a_first_frame_that_is_not_a_bind_is_refused_unread}`. The cap test was `waiting_streams_beyond_the_cap_are_closed_unread` until C1-1's fix (`2aff94e`). Streams still reading their `Bind` are bounded by the Tor listener: 4 per circuit × 64 live circuits |
| Capture starts only after the bind and stops on every exit path | VERIFIED | `a_capture_that_cannot_start_refuses_the_bind` (nothing shared, no indicator line), and the test source's drop flag in every served test |
| View-only input is never decoded or injected; control input is rate-capped and released when the session ends | VERIFIED | `view_only_input_is_never_decoded_or_injected` (undecodable bytes break nothing; no injector is opened), `control_input_is_capped_and_released_when_the_session_is_killed` |

A view-only session was ended by `tornect panic` on both ends. The driver's last check failed on client wording only; that was fixed (`543b17e`), and the check passed all 10 in session 7 (row "The live media check passes end to end" below). `docs/notes/m1-media-evidence.md` |

Wayland desktop, 3538×1918 (6.8 MP): 2.7–3.1 fps in two runs while capture alone reached 19 fps, so OpenH264's software encode on this 4-vCPU VM is the likely limit. Downscaling before the encode is the lever: OPEN (M5) |
| The desktop itself shared through the session layer on X11 | NEEDS-DEVICE(linux, X11 session) | The X11 backend is verified under Xvfb only; this VM's desktop is Wayland |
| The Wayland desktop shared through the session layer (`tornect host --capture desktop` on a Wayland session: the portal source, and for a control session the portal injector mapped over the encoded region) | VERIFIED the build VM 2026-09-22 (two live runs over real Tor) | The appendix driver of `docs/notes/m1-media-evidence.md`, adapted for the desktop: the host is started with `host --capture desktop`, the driver waits for `sharing: this desktop`, the card check is replaced by the host's `(wayland WxH; their input is injected)` note, and it still has 10 checks. Homes `s61-host`/`s61-client`; the restore token from the row above meant no dialog in any of the four sessions. **Run 2: `MEDIA LIVE CHECK PASS`, all 10 checks.** Control session: `MEDIA OK pictures=61 units=61 keyframes=1 errors=0 size=3538x1918 fps=2.7 kbps=133`. View-only session: `pictures=17 … errors=0 fps=3.0`. `tornect panic` ended media and session (`MEDIA ENDED by the host (the session ended)`, `SESSION ENDED by the host (PANIC)`); the host's PipeWire stream closed 100 ms after the kill and its portal session 172 ms after; the host exited cleanly. **Run 1:** 8 of 8 checks true (`pictures=72 … errors=0 fps=3.0 kbps=128`; view-only `pictures=18 … fps=3.1`), then FAIL on the client's media-end label (next row). Limits: `tornect connect` sends no input yet, so no input crossed Tor (pointer injection is verified by the row above); the 2.7–3.1 fps at 6.8 MP is most likely the software encoder on this 4-vCPU VM, since capture alone reached 19 fps |
| After `tornect panic`, the client labels the media end correctly | OPEN until one live panic run; fixed in code, VERIFIED by a unit test | In run 1 above, the client printed `MEDIA FAILED: media stream I/O: Circuit closed` and then `SESSION ENDED by the host (PANIC)`, and exited 0: `tor_cmd.rs` printed `MEDIA FAILED` for any viewer I/O error, even when the session end was the host's. With the Wayland source, the host writes its media `Close` only after capture has stopped and the portal session is closed (62–90 ms after the kill), and in that run the circuit was gone first. Run 2 got `MEDIA ENDED by the host (the session ended)`. The host side was right in both runs. **Fixed on the client** (`a338a9d`, M1-49): a media I/O error waits the same `BYE_GRACE` for the host's `BYE` as a `Close` does, and one under a host end is reported as the host's; `tor_cmd::tests::a_host_end_whose_media_circuit_closes_first_is_the_hosts` prints the exact run-1 line on the old code |
| GDI + `SendInput` through the session layer | NEEDS-DEVICE(windows) | `tornect.exe` is built (the last section, *The Windows build of the whole app*). Recipe: the Linux ↔ Windows row of the `serve_host` table with the Windows machine as the host (`tornect.exe host` captures the desktop by default), or `tornect.exe` (the window) on both sides; the viewer must show the Windows desktop, and its pointer and keys must act there |
| Review fixes (M1-44): a kill ends the session before any write to a stuck peer; a kill or a newly live session refuses a pending accept; unattended requests get `SESSION_ACTIVE`; a panicking sink still releases the session | VERIFIED (failing-first) | `fsm::tests::{a_kill_ends_the_session_while_a_write_to_the_peer_is_stuck, a_kill_while_the_popup_is_open_refuses_the_session, a_session_that_went_live_during_the_popup_blocks_the_accept, an_unattended_request_while_a_session_is_live_is_session_active, a_sink_that_panics_still_releases_the_session}`. All five failed on the old `fsm/host.rs`: one hang, three `SessionAccept`, one `live.count() == 1` |
| A host whose store was locked at start keeps a control channel (SLICE-B2 B2-2) | VERIFIED: fixed in `be17943` (M1-46) | OPEN at session 5: such a host ran with no IPC endpoint, and `tornect panic` said "no host is running here". The fix and its evidence are the two B2-2 rows of the next table. A Windows host still has no endpoint (M6), and `tornect panic` then names Ctrl-C |

## The xtask fix and the slice-C fixes (decisions M1-45 … M1-47; 2026-09-22, sixth and seventh sessions)

| Check | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| The gate and plain `cargo` no longer rebuild each other's Tor stack (M1-45) | VERIFIED | Measured both directions on the build VM: `cargo xtask lint-all` straight after a plain `cargo clippy` took 0.6 s with nothing rebuilt, and the plain `cargo clippy` straight after it took 0.3 s. Before the fix the same switch cost 33 s and a `ring` + `rustls` rebuild. Two unit tests in `xtask`, one of which fails on the old `cargo()` |
| `y`/`yes` at the host's session question does not grant control (SLICE-B2 B2-7) | VERIFIED | `parse_session` accepts only `a`/`allow` and `v`/`view`; `a_reflex_yes_does_not_grant_control` fails on the old code |
| The X11 grab refuses a geometry whose byte count would wrap (SLICE-B3 B3-7) | VERIFIED | `frame_bytes` is checked `usize` under a 2^27-pixel cap and runs before `GetImage`; `a_geometry_no_desktop_has_is_refused_rather_than_wrapped` panics with "attempt to multiply with overflow" on the old code |
| A host whose store was locked at start still ends its sessions on `tornect panic` (SLICE-B2 B2-2, M1-46) | VERIFIED | `a_host_whose_store_was_locked_still_answers_panic` fails on the old behaviour (no control channel). Runtime on the build VM with the real binary: store lock held by another process → `tornect panic` "Sessions ended", `unattended status` refused with the reason (0x0102), a second `tornect host` refused with the first one's pid |
| No CLI command runs offline beside a host that does not answer (SLICE-B2 B2-2) | VERIFIED (Linux) / NEEDS-DEVICE(windows) | `no_command_runs_offline_beside_a_host_that_does_not_answer` fails on the old routing. Runtime on the build VM with an unbindable socket path: the host warns that Ctrl-C ends its sessions; `tornect panic` and `unattended status` name the running host's pid. **Windows recipe:** start `tornect host`, then in a second console `tornect panic`: expect "a Tornect host is running for … but it has no control channel (the Windows one lands at M6); press Ctrl-C in its terminal", and the host's session line to say "Ctrl-C in this terminal ends it". A command already running offline when a host started still wrote beside it until `b2eb4ae` (SLICE-D D1, next table) |
| Idle or ghost media streams cannot keep a live session's viewer from binding (SLICE-C1 C1-1, M1-47) | VERIFIED | `squatting_streams_do_not_starve_a_live_sessions_bind`; on the old code the bind ends `Closed`. Until `5521d22` (SLICE-D D3) the test held only half the waiting slots and would have missed a live bind taking a slot. It now fills all 8 with ghost binds plus 4 idle streams, shows the set is full (one more ghost is refused `Busy` at once), then binds, and it fails (`Refused(1)`) when `MediaHost::serve` takes a slot before its look-up |
| A flooding host cannot starve what `tornect connect` joins the viewer with (SLICE-C2 C2-1) | VERIFIED (always-ready transport) / OPEN (real Tor flood) | `a_flooding_host_does_not_starve_what_the_viewer_is_joined_with` fails on the old viewer (its join partner never runs). Whether Arti's stream ever yields under a real flood was not measured, and no hostile-host flood has been run live |
| Leaving never waits on a host that stops reading (SLICE-B2 B2-8) | VERIFIED | `leaving_does_not_wait_on_a_host_that_stops_reading`: with the client's writes frozen, the old `bye` never returned; it now gives up after `timeouts::BYE` = 3 s (`6f27930`), and since `47b0dcd` (SLICE-D R9) the test asserts exactly that bound. The ping deadline held for M2 frames only: host `PING`s restarted the wait inside `next_event` until `73711f1` (SLICE-D D2, next table) |
| A crafted `i32::MIN` wheel delta is refused, not a panic (SLICE-C1 C1-5 (a)) | VERIFIED | `the_most_negative_wheel_delta_is_refused` panics with "attempt to negate with overflow" on the old code. C1-5 (b), the input bucket, is OPEN (next table) |
| A 20-unit window doubles the test card's frame rate over Tor (M1-47) | VERIFIED | Two live runs on the build VM (session 7): 309 and 296 pictures, 0 errors, in order, at 1280×720 and **13.2 / 13.0 fps**, against 6.3 fps with 8 units (session 5). The card's 15 fps is not reached; the pings measured 0.5–1.3 s RTT |
| Leaving waits until the host has read the `BYE` (M1-47) | VERIFIED | `leaving_waits_until_the_host_has_read_the_bye` fails on the old `bye`. In live run 1 of session 7 the client's flushed `BYE` was lost to its immediate node shutdown (the host's audit says `detail: io`); run 2, also before the fix, got it through (`the client left (USER)`). It was a race |
| The live media check passes end to end (session 7) | VERIFIED | Run 2: all 10 driver checks true: ping, SAS match, bind at 1280×720, pictures without error, card in order, the first client's exit, no second pairing, `tornect panic` ok, panic ends media and session, clean host exit. The driver in `m1-media-evidence.md` asked for 11 results from 10 checks and could never pass; fixed |
| Security review of `517a773..9bfbe87` (the session layer, the CLI media path, the M1-44 fixes) | VERIFIED: reviewed 2026-09-22 | The launch of 2026-09-21 died on the account's weekly usage limit with no report (`/home/mx/kore_project_V3/agents/security/reports/2026-09-22_tornect_slice_c_review_NOT_RUN.md`). Re-run 2026-09-22 (A′: three read-only threat modelers on the frozen worktree): 0 critical, 0 high, 2 medium, 6 low, 13 info, 90 candidates refuted. ## The window: egui GUI of the vertical slice (D10 fallback; 2026-09-23)

`tornect` with no subcommand (or `tornect gui [--capture test-card] [--no-host]`) opens it: a manual
winit loop driving egui-winit and egui-wgpu, never eframe (`apps/tornect/src/ui/`, slice.md §2).

| Check | Status | Evidence |

The operator then asked where the screen-sharing dialog was; a desktop notification at 16:20:59 named the Wayland host's window and its "Grant screen sharing" button. The console had no input after about 16:15:12 (Mutter `IdleMonitor` 1 729 647 ms at 16:44:01), no grant token and no `sessions.log` were written, and nothing crossed Tor between the windows: closed at 16:44 as **NOT PERFORMED**. On SIGTERM the two remaining windows logged `asked to quit by a signal: ending every session` and ended within 0.3 s. **Fifth attempt, 2026-09-24, at `996e6db`** (a fresh build; the homes kept, so the same IDs; only one host window on the desktop until the grant: the Wayland host and the `--no-host` client, with Xvfb `:99` and its zenity box off the desktop; the X11 host started only once the grant token existed): launched 19:39:30; the Wayland host started over the fourth attempt's `host.lock`, `ipc.sock` and `unattended.lock`; Tor connected directly in about 4.5 s; the Wayland host was `REACHABLE` at 19:40:43 (73 s). After the go at 19:42:44 the pairing request crossed Tor: the client connected at 19:43:26 (onion stream open at 19:43:33), the host logged `pair_requested` at 19:43:34 and, once the operator had compared the codes and clicked The codes match: pair (`pointer=true`), `pair_accepted` at 19:43:57. The operator then granted screen sharing (19:44:08, previous row) before answering the client's own code check, which ran out 50 s after the host's accept: at 19:44:47 the client said `VIEWER: Nothing was pinned; run the command again to retry.: aborted: pairing not confirmed` (a command-line hint, in a window), and at 19:44:48 the host said `paired with "…"; it left without asking for a session`. So the host trusts the client, the client pinned nothing, and no session was requested. Fixed since in `a4887c2` (the window's code question now stays up for up to 10 minutes and says each end in the window's words) and `f4bced7` (a device the host paired that then says nothing is reported as paired, not as a timeout); a live re-run is pending. The check was paused after the pairing, pending the operator. The three windows ended at 20:07:43–20:07:51 through their own close path (onion service stopped, Tor shut down, no signal line); the check sent them no signal. Control, pointer and keys, Stop, view only, the keys-only request and part 2's keys on Xvfb: **NOT PERFORMED** |

### The sharing indicator window (§B.6, UI_PLAN §6.3; 2026-09-23)

While any session runs, attended or unattended, control or view-only, a second small window titled
"Tornect: sharing" shows who, which mode and the clock, with **Stop**. It is always-on-top, has no
decorations, has a fixed size, sits at the top-right of the primary monitor and is not activated when
it opens (on X11 it is a utility window); the main window keeps its banner (`apps/tornect/src/ui/indicator.rs`). Its Stop is the
banner's Stop: the host-wide kill with `BYE{USER}`. Closing the window does nothing while the screen
is shared. A press-and-drag anywhere but Stop moves it.

| Check | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Appears at the first session, redraws on a mode change, disappears at the last end | VERIFIED (unit) | `ui::indicator::tests::{it_opens_at_the_first_session_and_closes_at_the_last_end, it_redraws_on_a_mode_change}`: open at the first session; redraw when a second overlaps and when one of two ends; close at the last end; open again for the next session. A mode change is capture reporting input injected or not injected, or a view-only session taking a control session's place; the clock ticking alone is no change. The model keeps every shared session in a list (`Model::sharing`), so a session never drops off the indicator while another overlaps it |
| Shows who, the mode and the clock; an unattended session says so; Stop is the banner's Stop, never the end of the viewed session | VERIFIED (unit, egui, no GPU) | `ui::indicator::tests::{it_shows_who_the_mode_and_the_clock_and_stop_stops_sharing, an_unattended_session_says_so, a_drag_elsewhere_moves_the_window}` read the drawn text ("SHARING "Laptop" · control · 01:05", then "view only" and "their input is ignored" after the change) and click Stop by its drawn position: the press yields nothing and the release yields exactly `StopSharing`. An unattended session's line starts with "UNATTENDED" instead of "SHARING" (§B.8.7.3 "**Unattended** · <name> · Control · [End]"; the banner too); `LiveSession::unattended` is carried into the model. A drag elsewhere yields `MoveIndicator` only. `ui::app::tests::the_indicators_stop_asks_to_stop_sharing`: `StopSharing` becomes `Command::StopSharing` and nothing else |
| Stop ends the right session, through the real FSM | VERIFIED (unit, real FSM) | `ui::backend::tests::the_indicators_stop_ends_the_shared_session_and_it_closes`: `wire` + `serve_signaling` + `client::connect` over a pipe, with the window's own consent and `GuiSessions`. The pairing popup is answered, then Allow control; the model then shows the session (label, control) and the indicator opens. `stop_sharing` (what `Command::StopSharing` runs) makes the client receive `BYE{USER}`; the session leaves the model and the indicator closes. A viewed session in the same model stays `Live` |
| The window asks for always-on-top, no decorations, a fixed size, no activation, top-right | VERIFIED (unit) | `ui::indicator::tests::{the_window_is_asked_to_stay_on_top_undecorated_and_unfocused, it_goes_to_the_top_right_of_the_monitor}`: `WindowLevel::AlwaysOnTop`, `decorations == false`, `resizable == false`, `active == false`, title "Tornect: sharing", 420×56 logical for one session. Position: 16 px from the right edge and 48 px from the top, below GNOME's top bar and a maximised window's caption buttons. It is scaled by the monitor, respects a monitor that does not start at 0,0, and never goes left of the monitor |
| On X11 the level request reaches the X server (preview on Xvfb, no network) | VERIFIED 2026-09-23 under Xvfb (no window manager) | `env -u WAYLAND_DISPLAY -u DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS DISPLAY=:97 XDG_SESSION_TYPE=x11 tornect gui --preview` on `Xvfb :97 -screen 0 2560x1200x24`, with a passive listener on the root window (x11rb, SubstructureNotify). The indicator window `"Tornect: sharing"` was mapped at 420x56+2124+48 (2560 − 420 − 16 = 2124) and sent `_NET_WM_STATE ADD _NET_WM_STATE_ABOVE`; the main and stream windows sent `REMOVE _NET_WM_STATE_ABOVE`. xprop: `_MOTIF_WM_HINTS = 0x2, 0x0, …` (no decorations); `WM_NORMAL_HINTS` min = max = 420 by 56, user-specified location 2124, 48; `_NET_WM_WINDOW_TYPE = _NET_WM_WINDOW_TYPE_UTILITY`. Screenshot: the red window with "SHARING "Laptop" · control · 00:11", "their keyboard and mouse act on this desktop" and Stop, beside the main window's banner and the stream window. Xvfb has no window manager, so nothing enforced the stacking there |
| X11 desktop: the window manager keeps it above the others; it does not take the focus; it survives the main window being minimised | NEEDS-DEVICE(linux X11 desktop with an EWMH window manager) | Recipe: on an X11 session, `tornect gui`, connect from a second machine, pair, Allow; or, without a network, `tornect gui --preview`. Type into another application while the indicator appears: the keystrokes stay there. winit has no "open unfocused" request on X11. The window is therefore typed as a utility window, which mutter does not focus when it maps (going by mutter's source as remembered, not re-read here); other window managers decide by their focus-stealing prevention. Minimise the main window and raise other windows over the top-right corner: the indicator stays visible and above them. Drag it by its background. Stop: the session ends and the window closes |
| Windows: `HWND_TOPMOST`, opens without activation, survives the main window being minimised | NEEDS-DEVICE(windows) | Built and linked into `tornect.exe` (the last section, *The Windows build of the whole app*), never run. Recipe: `tornect.exe gui` on Windows 10/11, connect from another machine, Allow. Keep typing in another application as the indicator appears: the focus stays there (`with_active(false)` shows it without activating it). Minimise the main window, then maximise another application: the indicator stays on top at the top-right, below that application's caption buttons. Stop: the session ends and the indicator closes |
| GNOME on Wayland: the level request is ignored; the window stays findable; GNOME shows its own screen-cast icon | NEEDS-DEVICE(linux console) | Wayland lets a client put its toplevel above the others only through layer-shell, which winit does not speak and GNOME does not offer. `AlwaysOnTop` is still requested, and GNOME ignores it, as it ignores the position. Recipe (with Phase B's step 5): after Allow, minimise the main window. The undecorated "Tornect: sharing" window stays up, not above the others. It is listed in the overview and Alt+Tab under that title and moves when dragged. GNOME's screen-cast icon shows in the top bar for as long as the portal stream runs. Stop: the session ends, the window closes and the icon goes. Not run: the three console attempts (row *Two windows over real Tor*) never reached a shared session, so the indicator never opened on GNOME |


## The Windows build of the whole app (`x86_64-pc-windows-gnullvm`; 2026-09-23)

Cross-built on the build VM: rustc 1.96.0 (LLVM 22.1.2), llvm-mingw 20260908 (clang 23.1.1, UCRT) with its
`bin` on `PATH`, `TMPDIR=$PWD/target/tmp`, 2 jobs, each build in its own `systemd-run --user --scope`
(systemd-oomd killed the terminal's builds twice under memory pressure). Commits `bd3ef35`, `0a1cd53`,
`818bb8a`, `787b5da`. Full evidence: `/home/mx/kore_project_V3/agents/classical_coder/reports/2026-09-23_windows_build.md`.

| Check | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Clippy for the target is clean on the whole workspace (`--all-targets -D warnings`), with no `result_large_err` allow | VERIFIED (lint) the build VM 2026-09-23 | `cargo clippy --target x86_64-pc-windows-gnullvm --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`: rc 0 at `bd3ef35`. Before it, `tornect-core` failed with 88 `result_large_err` (87 in the lib: `StoreError` 53, `GuardError` 34, `LimiterError` 2): an inline 88-byte `toml::de::Error` beside a `PathBuf`, which is 8 bytes larger on Windows, made `CoreError` 128 bytes there (120 on Linux). The TOML error is boxed. Then 4 dead-code errors in `tornect-platform`'s Unix-only IPC framing and `apps/tornect`'s `LINE_CAP`, now `#[cfg(unix)]` |
| The core error types stay small, and their text did not change | VERIFIED (unit: host, and the Windows binary under Wine) | `error::tests::error_types_stay_well_below_the_large_err_threshold`: `CoreError`, `StoreError`, `GuardError`, `LimiterError` ≤ 96 bytes; it failed on the old code (`CoreError is 120 bytes`). The gate lints the host only, so this test is the standing guard for Windows. `error::tests::parse_errors_keep_their_display_debug_and_source_text`: the golden Display, Debug and `source()` chain of `ConfigParse`, `HostsParse` and their three wrappers, captured from the old code; it passed before and after. Since the E2-3 fix (section *The slice-E review fixes*) the same test pins the new text, which no longer quotes the file |
| Every C and C++ object built for Windows has the stack protector (A3-F4, SLICE-C2 C2-3) | VERIFIED (build) the build VM 2026-09-23 | `0a1cd53`: `CFLAGS_x86_64_pc_windows_gnullvm` and `CXXFLAGS_x86_64_pc_windows_gnullvm` = `-fstack-protector-strong` (`.cargo/config.toml`). llvm-mingw's clang leaves it off by default (a test file: no `__stack_chk` reference without the flag). With `CC_ENABLE_DEBUG_OUTPUT=1`, every real compile line of the five C/C++ crates carries it: OpenH264 86/86, ring 16/16, zstd 25/25, liblzma 77/77, SQLite 1/1, e.g. `"x86_64-w64-mingw32-clang++" "-O3" … "-DX86_ASM" "-fstack-protector-strong" … "-c" "upstream/codec/decoder/plus/src/welsDecoderExt.cpp"`. `tornect.exe` defines `__stack_chk_fail` and `__stack_chk_guard` (llvm-mingw's `libmingwex`; the guard is seeded from `rand_s` at start). Not covered: OpenH264's 19 hand-written nasm kernels (nasm 3.01 is on the VM since 2026-09-23; on Linux too) |
| The whole app links for Windows, and `tornect.exe` needs no DLL beside it | VERIFIED (build) the build VM 2026-09-23 | `cargo build --release --target x86_64-pc-windows-gnullvm -p tornect` at `818bb8a`: rc 0 (6–8 min). `tornect.exe`: 145,409,024 bytes, `PE32+ executable for MS Windows 6.00 (console), x86-64`; 109 MB of it is DWARF line tables (`[profile.release] debug = "line-tables-only"`; `cargo xtask dist` removes them after the link with `strip --strip-debug`, which keeps the symbols. The `[profile.dist]` this row named was never used by dist and is gone: audit finding B30). It imports 33 DLLs, all Windows' own (kernel32, ntdll, user32, gdi32, dxgi, ws2_32, bcrypt, the UCRT's `api-ms-win-crt-*`, …). ring, the bundled SQLite, zstd, liblzma, OpenH264, winit and wgpu's dx12 backend compiled and linked unchanged. The one failure showed in the import table: the first link needed llvm-mingw's `libc++.dll` (`cc` asks for `c++`, a dylib); `818bb8a` links `libc++.a` |
| `tornect.exe` starts; `id` and `doctor` work | VERIFIED under Wine 10.0 on the build VM 2026-09-23 (not Windows) | A fresh Wine prefix under `target/tornect-test/`, no display, no Tor: `--version` → `tornect 0.1.0`; `id` with a fresh `TORNECT_HOME` generates a Tornect ID (a 56-character `.onion`) and a device ID, and a second `id` prints the same two; `doctor` → `tornect 0.1.0 (windows x86_64)`, `capture: gdi (GDI and SendInput)`, four directories `[ok]`, config `[defaults]`, both identities present, `sessions.log` 0 records; with `TORNECT_PT_DIR` at the pinned `lyrebird.exe`, `doctor` accepts it (the Windows build's SHA-256 pin matches). `cargo xtask pt-fetch` and `cargo xtask dist` (`xtask/src/{pt,dist,sha256}.rs`; `docs/RELEASING.md`,
`packaging/pt/SOURCES.md`). Runs on the build VM, each heavy one in its own `systemd-run --user --scope` with
2 jobs; none was killed. Full evidence: `/home/mx/kore_project_V3/agents/devops/reports/2026-09-23_dist.md`.
