A failed `schtasks /Query` means "not registered" only when `<system directory>\Tasks\Tornect` reads NotFound (`symlink_metadata`); present or access-denied means a task this account cannot read, reported as such with the ELEVATED readiness warning; any other answer is an error. `uninstall` deletes an elevated or unreadable task through the UAC prompt only, then reports "removed" only on a definite not-found, else fails (`ok: false` in `sessions.log`) with "remove it in an administrator console with `schtasks /Delete /TN Tornect /F`". `install` never answers "nothing starts at login" after a registration: "registered; this account cannot read it back". Recipe: (1) a standard account with no task: `.\tornect.exe service status` → "nothing starts at login"; (2) register the task from an administrator console (`service install`, and again with the elevated option, since removed), then in the standard console `service status` → "a task \Tornect exists that this account cannot read …", readiness `warn: … ELEVATED …`; `service uninstall` → the password dialog, then the UAC prompt, then "nothing starts at login", and `schtasks /Query /TN Tornect` in an administrator console fails; (3) the same for a filtered administrator (UAC on, not elevated); (4) record what `symlink_metadata` returns for a present and a missing task for both accounts: if a missing task reads access-denied, the code fails safe (an unneeded UAC prompt and a "could not read" row, never "removed"), and this row must say so. At M6: the Task Scheduler COM API (`ITaskFolder::GetTask`: `0x80070002` absent, `E_ACCESSDENIED` present) replaces the file test |
| `service install` with the elevated option is refused to a standard account, before any dialog (the operator's ruling of 2026-09-24) | SUPERSEDED 2026-09-29: the elevated option is gone (`f80b4e8`, M-FEAT-3 section below); was VERIFIED (unit, a fake Task Scheduler) 2026-09-24; NEEDS-DEVICE(windows) | `autostart::tests::a_standard_account_never_gets_the_elevated_task` (failing first: on the old code a standard account's elevated install went on to the UAC prompt and ended `Registered { elevated: true, unreadable: true, note: "registered; this account cannot read it back" }`) and `only_an_administrator_account_passes_the_elevated_check`. The check (`win::is_administrator`): `TokenElevationType`; for a filtered token (`Limited`) its linked full token, otherwise the process token, is asked `CheckTokenMembership` against `WinBuiltinAdministratorsSid`; a failed check refuses. The backend's `preflight` runs it, so the refusal (`G_PRECONDITION`) comes before the password dialog; `task_install` runs it again before the UAC prompt. Recipe, while the option existed: (1) a standard account: `.\tornect.exe service install` with the elevated option → "… needs an administrator account, and this is a standard account …" with no password dialog and no UAC prompt, and `schtasks /Query /TN Tornect` in an administrator console finds nothing; (2) a filtered administrator (UAC on, console not elevated): the password dialog, then the UAC prompt; (3) an administrator in an elevated console and, if at hand, one with UAC off: the same, no refusal; (4) a member of Backup Operators only: refused |
| Slice-F F2-3: `service install` through a running host is refused before the dialog (the host's backend was built from the host's own executable when it started); `uninstall` still goes through it | VERIFIED (unit) 2026-09-24 | `unattended::tests::a_running_host_refuses_service_install_but_uninstalls` (failing first: on the old code "the host route installed (elevated: false)"): a host-route context (`kill: Some`) answers `ServiceInstall` (plain and elevated) with `G_PRECONDITION` "a Tornect host is running; stop it (`systemctl --user stop tornect-host`, or Ctrl-C in its terminal) and run `tornect service install` again: it registers the tornect you run", with no re-auth call, no backend call and no `AutostartChanged` line; `ServiceUninstall` through the host removes the registration and adds "the running host keeps running until you stop it". Windows has no host route (no control channel until M6): every Windows change runs offline and `route()` refuses beside a running host (default 2). P-1 applies: the offline route has no 60 s cool-down after a stop (M6) |
| Slice-F F2-4: while the keystore cannot be read, a start-at-login change or a backup export is refused rather than authorised with a log line that is only buffered | VERIFIED (unit) 2026-09-24 | `unattended::tests::f2_4_logged_external_changes_wait_for_a_readable_log_key` (`tornect-core`; failing first: on the old code "ServiceInstall { elevated: false } authorised while its log line could only be buffered"): with `K_master` present but its read failing (`FailedClosed(KeystoreLocked)`), `authorize` refuses `ServiceInstall` (plain and elevated), `ServiceUninstall` and `BackupExport` with `G_KEYSTORE_LOCKED` before any re-auth call, and the same `ServiceUninstall` goes through once `retry_load` finds the keystore readable. The check runs before the dialog and again before the line. `G_KEYSTORE_LOCKED`, not the review's `G_PRECONDITION`: it is the code this crate gives the same store state everywhere else (`GuardError::from(StoreError::NotLoaded(FailedClosed(KeystoreLocked)))`), and the CLI's hint for it says the keystore could not be unlocked. Operations that log nothing (identity view, settings, backup import) are not refused. A removal is refused too, so its refusal says first how to remove the registration by hand ("the keystore cannot be read, so this removal could not be logged and was refused; remove it by hand with `systemctl --user disable --now tornect-host.service`, then delete <the unit file>"; on Windows "remove it in an administrator console with `schtasks /Delete /TN Tornect /F`"): `unattended::tests::an_uninstall_the_keystore_refuses_names_the_manual_removal` (a real master key made unreadable; failing first: the old refusal named no step and was cut at 256 characters) and `autostart::tests::the_manual_removal_names_the_unit_and_its_file` |
| Slice-F F1-2: the task definition file is written where only this user's programs can change it, and what the Task Scheduler registered is compared with what was written | VERIFIED (unit, a fake Task Scheduler) 2026-09-24; NEEDS-DEVICE(windows) | The file goes to `<FOLDERID_LocalAppData>\Tornect\tornect-autostart-<random>.xml` (from `SHGetKnownFolderPath`, never `%TEMP%`), created new and deleted after `/Create`. After the registration `task_install` reads the task back and `registered_matches` compares the principal (this account: its SID, or a name `LookupAccountNameW` resolves to it), the run level (absent = `LeastPrivilege`) and the command (case-folded) with its arguments; a task that differs is deleted, through the UAC prompt when it or the install was elevated, and the install fails naming what differed. `autostart::tests::a_task_registered_other_than_written_is_removed` (failing first: on the old code a task registered with the command `"C:\Users\Public\evil.exe" host` was kept, "Command (elevated: false) was kept: Ok(Registered(…))") and `registered_matches_finds_every_swap`. N-3: the compare also requires exactly one action in each definition, of any kind (a second `<Exec>` or a `<ComHandler>` after Tornect's would run too): `a_task_with_a_second_action_is_not_the_one_written` (failing first: a definition with a second `<Exec>` matched). P2-I2: the count and the field reads trust the scheduler's canonical XML, so a definition that holds a comment, CDATA section, DOCTYPE or processing instruction after its one XML declaration (the fixture's `<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-16"?>` is accepted) is not the one written: `markup_that_could_hide_an_action_is_not_the_one_written` (failing first: `<![CDATA[ > <x> ]]>` in the first action hid a second `<Exec>`). NEEDS-DEVICE: the exact declaration `schtasks /Query /XML` prints; a form other than `<?xml` + white space + attributes + `?>` fails the install closed. A task this account cannot read back is reported as before ("registered; this account cannot read it back, so it was not compared with what Tornect wrote"). Recipe: `tornect service install` (and the elevated install as a filtered administrator, while it existed) must succeed and `service status` show the command; record what `schtasks /Query /TN Tornect /XML` prints as the principal's `<UserId>` (a SID or a name, for a local account and a Microsoft account): if it is a form `LookupAccountNameW` cannot resolve, every install fails closed and this row must say so. At M6: an owner-only DACL and a held handle, or registration through COM with no file |
| Slice-F F1-5: the elevated task cannot be started on demand | VERIFIED (unit) 2026-09-24; NEEDS-DEVICE(windows) | `autostart::tests::only_the_plain_task_starts_on_demand` (failing first: on the old code the elevated XML held `<AllowStartOnDemand>true</AllowStartOnDemand>`): `AllowStartOnDemand` is `false` for the `HighestAvailable` task and `true` for the plain one. Recipe, while the elevated option existed: after an elevated `service install`, `schtasks /Run /TN Tornect` from the user's console must fail and the task must still start at the next logon. The `tor.pt_path` steering of an elevated host through `config.toml` stays (disclosed; M6 with E2-8) |
| E3-3: a release fetches the advisory database first, and the gate and each zip name it | VERIFIED the build VM 2026-09-24 | `cargo deny fetch` (03:01) moved the RustSec database from `b331df68…` (2026-08-29) to `1931a145168d457fd79b321277b25b0e51777157` (2026-09-23 21:20:53 UTC). Since `37d04fe` the gate prints `cargo deny check (offline; advisory DB 1931a145168d of 2026-09-23 21:20:53 UTC)`, and `dist` runs the same check before it builds, refuses without a database, and writes into each BUILD-INFO.txt `advisories    RustSec advisory-db <commit>, committed <date> UTC, <n> days before the source commit` (seen in both zips of the dist check) and repeats it on its last line for the release notes. Unit tests `tests::the_advisory_database_is_named_by_commit_and_date`, `dist::tests::the_advisory_line_names_the_database_and_its_age`. `docs/RELEASING.md` step 1 fetches the database before the gate |
| RUSTSEC-2026-0285: rustls 0.23.43 accepted TLS 1.3 handshake messages across a key change within one record (GHSA-2mjx-qc3c-rqvc; CVSS 3.1 5.3) | FIXED `00bc286` 2026-09-24 | Found by the refreshed database: the gate failed with `advisories FAILED` (rustls 0.23.43, `Cargo.lock:432`). Reached on both targets by every Tor channel: `rustls` ← `futures-rustls 0.26.0` ← `tor-rtcompat 0.45.0` ← Arti ← `tornect-signal` ← `tornect` (Arti's TLS client, TLS 1.3 on). The pin is now `=0.23.45`, the first fixed release; `Cargo.lock` changed only in rustls' own entry (version and checksum); gate 609 passed, 0 failed, 5 ignored against the fresh database |
| `cargo clippy --target x86_64-pc-windows-gnullvm --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, xtask included | VERIFIED the build VM 2026-09-24 | With llvm-mingw's `bin/` on `PATH`. Before `fbb846d`: `error: function `leak` is never used` (`xtask/src/pt.rs:620`, a test helper whose only caller is `#[cfg(unix)]`). After it: rc 0, no warning |
| The dist check of 2026-09-24 at `594711b` (both platforms, one run) | VERIFIED the build VM 2026-09-24 (its zips deleted: not the release) | Clean tree and the same HEAD before and after the build (E3-1); Linux built in 372 s and Windows in 433 s (the rustls bump rebuilt the Tor stack), 2 jobs, no kill; `sha256sum -c SHA256SUMS` OK. Each zip holds 8 files: `BUILD-INFO.txt`, `LICENSE`, `README-release.md`, `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.txt`, `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`, `pt/lyrebird[.exe]`, `pt/lyrebird.txt`, `tornect[.exe]`. Both BUILD-INFO.txt name the commit without "WITH UNCOMMITTED CHANGES", the cargo-deny check and the advisory database, and a good signature. Linux zip 25,191,724 B, Windows 21,436,695 B. Linux from the unpacked zip: `--version`, `id` (created, then the same ID), `doctor` rc 0 with the zip's `pt/lyrebird` |
| The Windows zip under Wine with the home inside the Wine profile (`docs/RELEASING.md` step 4, since `b09d919`) | VERIFIED under Wine on the build VM 2026-09-24 (not Windows) | From the unpacked zip of the dist check, through `wine cmd /v:on /c '… & echo exit !ERRORLEVEL!'`: `tornect.exe --version` → `tornect 0.1.0`, exit 0; `--home %USERPROFILE%\tornect-a24-h1 id --json` → `"created":true`, then `"created":false`, exit 0; `doctor` → `capture: gdi (GDI and SendInput)` and the zip's `pt\lyrebird.exe`, exit 0. A home outside the profile (`--home ..\a24-outside-home`, `Z:\…`) is refused: "is not inside your user folder (C:\users\<user>) … (review finding E2-2)", exit 1, nothing created. The rows above that report `ERRORLEVEL=0` through `cmd /c "… & echo %ERRORLEVEL%"` read ERRORLEVEL when cmd parsed the line, before `tornect.exe` ran; `/v:on` with `!ERRORLEVEL!` reads it after |
| L-1: the licence file takes only each package's own regular licence files | VERIFIED (unit) 2026-09-24 | `5b3b02e`: `own_files` reads directory entries without following links and refuses a symbolic link among the licence files or inside `LICENSES/`; `crate_file` accepts only a relative path of normal components that stays inside the package, checks every component with `symlink_metadata`, and requires a regular file of at most 1 MiB; every text is capped at 1 MiB, and control characters other than the tab are dropped. `licences::tests::a_package_brings_only_its_own_regular_licence_files` builds crafted packages in a temporary directory (failing first: the old code accepted all five: a symbolic-link `LICENSE`, a symbolic-link `LICENSES/` directory, an absolute and a climbing `license-file`, a text of 1 MiB + 1 byte, and it kept escape characters); each now fails with its reason. Exposure before the fix was none: no package of `Cargo.lock` has a symbolic link near its root, a `LICENSES/` directory or a `license-file` (Security phase 2c) |
| L-2: the gate and dist name exactly the database copy cargo-deny checks | VERIFIED (unit) 2026-09-24 | `advisory_db()` reads the one copy under `$CARGO_HOME/advisory-dbs/` whose `.git/config` origin is `https://github.com/RustSec/advisory-db`, and refuses none, two or more copies, or a single copy of another URL (`tests::only_a_single_rustsec_copy_is_named`, with fake copies); `tests::deny_toml_keeps_the_default_advisory_database` fails if `deny.toml` ever sets `db-path` or `db-urls`. Before, the gate took the newest of all copies. On the build VM: one copy, `advisory-db-3157b0e258782691`, and the gate names it as before |
| I-3: BUILD-INFO.txt and the release-notes lines name the advisories deny.toml ignores, with their reasons | VERIFIED (unit) 2026-09-24 | `ignored_advisories` reads `[advisories] ignore` of `deny.toml` (each `{ id, reason }` on one line; an entry without a reason is refused): RUSTSEC-2023-0071, RUSTSEC-2024-0436, RUSTSEC-2026-0192 (`tests::deny_toml_ignores_are_read_with_their_reasons`). BUILD-INFO.txt's *Checked before the build* gains an `ignored` line per advisory, and dist prints `ignored by deny.toml (for the release notes): <id>: <reason>`. `dist::tests::build_info_names_every_ignored_advisory_with_its_reason` failed on the old lines and passes now |
| I-2: dist refuses an advisory database more than 7 days old | VERIFIED (unit) 2026-09-24 | `dist` prints `the advisory database <commit> is <n> days old (dist's limit: 7)` and stops above 7 days unless `--allow-old-advisory-db` is given (then a WARNING in that line; the release notes must say so). The age counts from the database's commit time to the build machine's clock; a clock behind the database counts as 0. `dist::tests::an_old_advisory_database_is_refused_unless_allowed` failed before the limit (8 days accepted) and passes now; not run live (a dist run is the final build's step) |

## The slice-E review fixes (2026-09-23)

The review of `0e3afea..2480dfb`: `/home/mx/kore_project_V3/agents/security/reports/2026-09-23_tornect_slice_e_review.md`. Each fix below was written test first: the test failed on the code before it and passes after.

| Check | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| **E1-1 ≡ E2-1 (MEDIUM)**: keys typed for another program never answer a card, a click cannot land on an accept that just appeared, and a card never takes the keyboard focus (decision M1-50, "pointer-only Allow") | VERIFIED (unit, egui harness); the Windows foreground behaviour NEEDS-DEVICE(windows) | A question or a session start asks for attention only: `request_user_attention(Critical)`, and `set_minimized(false)` only for a window known to be minimized (on X11 that call is itself a `_NET_ACTIVE_WINDOW` activation); never `focus_window` (`ui::app::tests::the_wake_asks_for_attention_without_taking_the_focus`, through a seam that records the window calls). In every card the refusal comes first (Deny, Refuse, No) and answers the keyboard at once (`deny_is_the_first_key_stop_and_answers_at_once`); the accepts (Allow control, View only, The codes match: pair, Yes, the codes match) are never in the Tab order (`Sense::CLICK`), answer only a primary click of the pointer (`clicked_by(Primary)`, never Space or Enter), and stay disabled for 1 s after the card appears and after it moves. `ui::view::tests::{keys_alone_never_allow_a_session, keys_alone_never_pair, keys_alone_never_confirm_the_codes, even_after_a_click_in_the_window_keys_never_allow}` (1 to 5 Tabs, then Space or Enter), `a_click_in_the_cards_first_second_does_nothing` (a click 300 ms after the card appeared does nothing; at 1.6 s it allows, with `pointer: true`) and `a_card_that_moves_waits_another_second` (the card above goes; the session card below moves up and waits again). Before the fix: 1 Tab then Space allowed a session, 2 Tabs then Space paired, and the 300 ms click allowed. Recipe (Windows): keep typing in another program while a paired device asks for a session; Tornect's taskbar button flashes, the typing stays in the other program, and Tab, Space in Tornect reach only Deny. The live check on the GNOME console was **NOT PERFORMED** (2026-09-23 22:53–23:38, 2026-09-24 01:14–01:20 and 16:12–16:44): in the first two nobody was at the console, and at 16:12–16:44 the first step (the grant) was never taken, so no session was ever requested (row "Two windows over real Tor"). The fifth attempt (2026-09-24 19:42–19:50, at `996e6db`) got the grant, and the host's pairing card was answered by a pointer click (`pointer=true`), but the client's code check ran out, so again no session was requested, and the check was paused after the pairing: the keys-only request is **NOT PERFORMED** |
| **N-1 (LOW; a residual of E1-1, from the fix-closure check)**: a card armed before the window was minimized is not live the moment the window comes back | VERIFIED (unit, egui harness) 2026-09-24; NEEDS-DEVICE(windows) | `Form::window_came_back` restarts every card's arming second; the app calls it when a question's attention request un-minimizes the window (`ask_attention` now says whether it did) and when the main window gets the focus or stops being hidden (`WindowEvent::Focused(true)`, `Occluded(false)`). `ui::view::tests::a_window_that_comes_back_arms_its_cards_again` (failing first: on the old code "the old card answered 300 ms after the window came back: [Session { id: 7, decision: AllowOnce, pointer: true }]"): an armed session card, the window back, a click 300 ms later answers nothing, one after the new second allows; `ui::app::tests::{the_wake_asks_for_attention_without_taking_the_focus, focus_and_unhiding_bring_the_window_back}`. Consequence: a click that brings the Tornect window to the front never answers a card (click again after a second). Recipe (Windows): a session card up, minimize the window without answering, have a second device send a pairing request, and click where "Allow control" reappears within a second: nothing is answered; record whether the restored window came up above the foreground one |
| **E1-6 (LOW)**: a release (key-up, button-up, `ReleaseAll`) is never dropped on the way to the host, so no key or button stays down after a Tor stall | VERIFIED (unit, both ends) | Host (`tornect-session`): an input is decoded first, and a release (`InputEvent::is_release`) skips the 250/s token bucket; `Tracked` injects a release only for what it holds, so a flood of them injects nothing. `tests::releases_are_never_dropped_by_the_input_budget`: a burst of 600 (a key and a button down, 596 moves, the button and the key up) against a bucket of 500 that never refills gives 502 injected, 98 moves dropped, and both releases while the session goes on (before: 500 injected, the releases dropped). Window: a release that finds the 256-deep queue full is owed as a `ReleaseAll`, sent before anything else once there is room (`StreamLink::send`), or by the idle loop every 50 ms (`StreamLink::settle`). `ui::backend::tests::a_release_that_finds_the_queue_full_goes_out_as_release_all` (before: the next move came out and the key-up was lost); `tornect_input` `tests::releases_are_key_ups_button_ups_and_release_all` |
| **E1-4 (LOW)**: keys typed while the viewer still waits for the first picture are not sent | VERIFIED (unit) | `Forwarder::key` drops a press until a picture has been drawn (`set_picture(Some(_))`), as a pointer press off the picture is dropped; a key-up always goes out (the host's `Tracked` drops one for a key it never saw pressed). `ui::stream::tests::keys_wait_for_the_first_picture` (before: both presses were sent and would have been injected at bind, on a screen the viewer had not seen) |
| **E1-3 (LOW)**: a new session's stream window never shows the previous session's last picture | VERIFIED (unit) | `FrameSlot::clear` forgets a picture not drawn yet; `SlotSink::new` (the start of every viewed session) clears the slot, and so does the window whenever the stream window goes (closed, session over, quitting). `ui::stream::tests::a_new_session_never_shows_the_last_ones_picture` (before: the stale picture was handed to the next session's first draw, which also armed pointer input over it) |
| **E1-2 (LOW)**: a long or crafted pinned label cannot push the banner's Stop out of the window, hide the mode or the clock, or pass for them; the pairing name field shows what an accept pins | VERIFIED (unit) | Banner: laid out from the right (Stop, the clock, then the line cut to the room left); the line says the mode before the label (`SHARING · sees and controls this screen · "<label>"`); the tooltip has the whole label. Indicator: `SHARING · control · 01:05 · "<label>"`, the label last. Both show at most 24 characters of the label. The pairing card's name field starts with the declared name (a re-pair: the pinned label, which the store keeps). `ui::view::tests::{a_long_label_keeps_the_banners_stop_in_the_window, the_name_field_starts_with_the_declared_name}`, `ui::indicator::tests::a_long_label_cannot_hide_or_imitate_the_mode_and_the_clock`, with the 64-character label `Ana" · view only · 00:04 xxx…`. Before: Stop was drawn at x 631–712 in the 560-point window, the indicator read `SHARING  "Ana" · view only · 00:04 xxx…" · control · 01:05`, and an accept without typing sent `label: None` |
| **E1-5 (LOW), the text only**: the home screen promises only what is true while unattended grants can start sessions | VERIFIED (unit) | The hosting line now reads "Incoming requests: reachable; paired devices ask before they see this screen, unless you granted one unattended access" (was "…; you are asked before anyone sees this screen"). `ui::view::tests::the_hosting_line_promises_only_what_is_true`. OPEN: the §B.8.7.3 badge with the number of devices that can connect without asking, which reads the guard's store (after the service work) |
| **E2-3 (LOW; the claim confirmed by reading)**: a TOML parse error never carries the file's text, so a private bridge line in `config.toml` reaches neither `tornect doctor`'s output nor a failing command's exit | VERIFIED (unit) | Confirmed first: `toml::de::Error`'s `Display` quotes the whole offending line (toml 1.1.4 `de/error.rs:117-140`) and its `Debug` holds the whole input (`input: Some(…)`); serde's message quotes the value; `doctor` printed `{e}` (`main.rs:474`) and a failing command prints every source's `Display`. Now the four parse sites (`Config::load`, the two trust stores, `hosts.toml`) keep only a `TomlError`: line, column, the key when the file names it plainly (`tor.bridge_lines`), and the message with every quoted or backticked value before its ", expected" elided. `error::tests::a_parse_error_never_shows_the_files_text` (a bridge line written as a string, and one with a stray token: no "203.0.113.7", "cert=" or "iat-mode" in any Display, Debug or source, and the error still names line 2; before: the Display held the whole line) and `error::tests::parse_errors_keep_their_display_debug_and_source_text` (the new texts, e.g. `line 2, column 11 (media.max_fps): invalid type: string "…", expected u32`) |
| **E2-2 (LOW)**: on Windows a `--home` or `TORNECT_HOME` outside the user's profile folder is refused, so Tornect's keys, `config.toml` and stores never land where other local accounts can read them (its directories get no ACL of their own until M6) | VERIFIED (unit) 2026-09-24; NEEDS-DEVICE(windows) | `autostart::tests::a_windows_home_is_taken_only_inside_the_profile`: `windows_home_ok` accepts the profile folder and anything under it (case-folded), and refuses `C:\tornect-home`, `C:\Users\Public\…`, a sibling profile (`C:\Users\meX`), a `..` escape, another drive, UNC and verbatim (`\\?\`) paths, and any home when the OS names no profile. `main` runs it (Windows only) on `--home`/`TORNECT_HOME` after `std::path::absolute`, before any command; the profile is `SHGetKnownFolderPath(FOLDERID_Profile)` (`tornect_platform::user_profile`), never the environment. The refusal and the start-at-login task's are one literal (`WINDOWS_HOME_RULE`). No failing-first run: the rule is new (the review named no test). Under Wine 10.0 on the build VM 2026-09-24 (the release exe of `0aaf4e0`, the prefix under `target/tornect-test/`): `--home 'C:\users\<user>\rf-h1' id` → an ID, `doctor` rc 0; `--home 'Z:\…\target\tornect-test\rf-wine-outside' id` and `TORNECT_HOME='C:\tornect-h1' id` → refused with the text above, rc 1, neither directory created. Recipe: `.\tornect.exe --home C:\tornect-h1 id` → refused, naming `C:\tornect-h1`, the profile and "(review finding E2-2)", nothing created under `C:\tornect-h1`; `.\tornect.exe --home %USERPROFILE%\tornect-h1 id` → an ID. Residual (review N-2): a junction under the profile that points elsewhere passes the prefix test |
| **E2-4 (LOW) and note P-2**: no DLL is loaded by name from the current directory, `PATH` or the executable's folder at run time, and `credui.dll` is not a load-time import | VERIFIED (unit + gnullvm clippy) 2026-09-24 for run-time loads and `credui.dll`; the load-time imports: the dependent-load flag set in `37a70e1`, checked on the release exe by the final `dist`, the loader's effect NEEDS-DEVICE(windows) | **Load-time imports:** seven that are not KnownDLLs, `bcrypt`, `bcryptprimitives`, `dwmapi`, `dxgi`, `pdh`, `powrprof` and `uxtheme` (the import table of the release exe of 2026-09-24), are resolved by the loader before `main` can restrict anything, from the executable's folder first unless the image says otherwise. `37a70e1` sets the linker's `--dependent-load-flag=0x800` (`LOAD_LIBRARY_SEARCH_SYSTEM32`) for the gnullvm target: llvm-mingw's `ld.lld` accepts it and the CRT's load configuration has the field (the round-1 closure: intent and syntax sound). The final `dist` checks `DependentLoadFlags: 0x800` on the stripped release exe (`llvm-readobj --coff-load-config`). Until a Windows machine shows that the loader honours it, keep `tornect.exe` in the folder its zip unpacks to, never in one other accounts can write to. NEEDS-DEVICE: whether the loader applies an executable's own flag to its imports (a planted `dwmapi.dll` beside the exe loads without the flag and not with it; Process Monitor). **Closed:** `main` first calls `SetDefaultDllDirectories(LOAD_LIBRARY_SEARCH_SYSTEM32)` (`tornect_platform::restrict_dll_search`; a failure stops Tornect). The GUI's wgpu instances use `Dx12Compiler::Fxc` (`d3dcompiler_47.dll`, in the system directory since Windows 10) and take no DX12 option from `WGPU_DX12_*` (no Agility SDK path): `ui::app::tests::the_gpu_never_loads_a_shader_compiler_by_name`. CredUI's two entry points come from `LoadLibraryExW("credui.dll", LOAD_LIBRARY_SEARCH_SYSTEM32)` + `GetProcAddress` for each prompt (it covers credui's own dependencies too), no longer from windows-sys's `raw-dylib` import, which the loader resolved before `main` from the executable's folder first (`credui.dll` is not a KnownDLL). The release exe of `0aaf4e0` (`cargo build --release --target x86_64-pc-windows-gnullvm -p tornect`, 2026-09-24): `llvm-readobj --coff-imports` lists 33 DLLs and no `credui.dll`; under Wine 10.0 it starts with the restriction in place (`--version`, `id`, `doctor` rc 0; a failed `SetDefaultDllDirectories` would have stopped it), and `service install` with the elevated option passed the administrator check (Wine's account is an administrator) and was refused by the Program Files rule, with no dialog. Wine is not Windows. Recipe: (1) `llvm-readobj --coff-imports tornect.exe` names no `credui.dll`; (2) copy `tornect.exe` into a folder that also holds a `credui.dll` and a `d3dcompiler_47.dll` that are not Windows' (for example renamed copies of another DLL): the window opens and paints, and `tornect service install` raises Windows' own secure password prompt, with neither planted DLL loaded (Process Monitor or `listdlls`); (3) the GPU check: the window opens on an NVIDIA, an AMD and an Intel machine (a driver whose user-mode part loads a sibling DLL from its DriverStore folder by name would now fail; none is known); (4) while the elevated option existed, an elevated `service install` from a filtered administrator: the UAC prompt appears and the task is registered (`ShellExecuteExW` "runas" after the restriction) |
| **E2-7 (INFO)**: the log lines of a pairing and a session name the peer by a short form: the host by the first eight characters of its address (`TornectId::short`), a device by its fingerprint (`DeviceId`'s `Debug`) | VERIFIED (unit) 2026-09-24 | The client's three lines (`session accepted`, `host paired`, `unattended session accepted`) log `host = %cfg.host.short()`; the host FSM's six lines and the unattended module's three log `peer = ?device`. `fsm::tests::the_logs_name_peers_by_short_forms_only` runs a pairing and a session under a capturing subscriber at TRACE and finds neither the host's full address (with or without `.onion`) nor the device's full ID, and finds both short forms (failing first: on the old lines "the host's address appears in full in the logs"). The capture is one process-wide subscriber that writes only a capturing thread's lines: the first, thread-local one (`set_default`) missed lines whenever another test's thread reached a call site first, since tracing caches a call site's interest process-wide and, with one dispatcher registered, asks the reaching thread's (4 of 21 full runs of the binary failed; 0 of 20 after; `a_call_site_first_reached_on_another_thread_is_still_captured` pins the cause and failed 5 of 5 with the old capture). The host's own address stays in its own `onion service launched`/`stopped` lines, as it does on its `Tornect ID:` line |
| The window ends its sessions gracefully on SIGINT and SIGTERM (Ctrl-C and Ctrl-Break on Windows), and what a killed host leaves behind never blocks the next start | VERIFIED (unit) 2026-09-24; live on the build VM's GNOME console 2026-09-24 without a session (SIGTERM; the stale files); SIGINT and a signal during a live session NEEDS-DEVICE | In the last live GUI runs the window ignored SIGINT (winit's loop) and died on SIGTERM, with a session live and `state/host.lock` and `state/ipc.sock` left behind. Now a tokio task waits for the signal (`ui::quit_requested`) and wakes the event loop with `Wake::Quit`, which takes the close button's path (`App::quit`: every session ended with a goodbye, at most 10 s). On that path the hosted sessions end first, then the viewed session's goodbye gets up to 6 s (review N-4: in the other order a remote controller kept this desktop for those seconds when Tor stalled the goodbye): `ui::backend::tests::closing_ends_the_hosted_session_before_the_viewed_goodbye` (failing first: with the old order the host-wide kill had not fired 200 ms into a stalled goodbye). A kill ends only the sessions armed before it, so the shutdown fires it again every 100 ms while the goodbye waits, once more after it, and on every pass of the 3 s grace (the CLI host's grace too): a session that goes live after the first kill (a peer with unattended rights, a consent clicked while the window closes) ends within 100 ms (review P2-I4): `a_session_that_goes_live_during_the_goodbye_is_ended_too` (failing first: a guard armed 200 ms into a stalled goodbye was not killed within 1 s). `ui::tests::a_terminate_signal_is_a_quit_request` sends the test process a real SIGTERM and sees the request (the process survives it). `host::tests::what_a_killed_host_leaves_behind_never_blocks_the_next`: with a stale `host.lock` (a dead pid) and a stale `ipc.sock` (a socket nobody listens on) in place, `HostLock::acquire` and the control channel both come up: the lock is an OS lock that dies with its process, and a socket that refuses connections is replaced; the CLI host's clean exit also leaves both files (live run in the service row), with the same result. Not handled: the Windows console's close button, logoff and shutdown events (E2-6, disclosed). Recipe: start `tornect` (the window) hosting, open a session from a second device, then `kill -TERM <pid>` (and, in another run, Ctrl-C in the terminal that started it): the viewer gets the session's end, the window closes within 10 s, and `sessions.log` has the session's end record; start the window again at once: it hosts. Live on the build VM 2026-09-24 (the console check's fourth attempt, `37a70e1`): the windows started at 16:04:20 in homes where the `6b0da44` windows' SIGTERM at 01:21 had left `host.lock`, `unattended.lock` and `ipc.sock`; both hosts logged `IPC endpoint listening`, took the lock (the new pid in `host.lock`) and published their old IDs. SIGTERM at 16:44:21 to the Wayland host and the client (no session live): both logged `asked to quit by a signal: ending every session`, stopped the onion service and Tor and ended within 0.3 s; the three files stay on disk after that exit too. Not run live: SIGINT, and a signal during a live session |

## The slice-G review fixes (2026-09-24)

The review of `d1ef55b..0e3afea` (slice G): `/home/<login>/kore_project_V3/agents/security/reports/2026-09-24_tornect_slice_g_review.md`;
its closure check, round 1: `…/2026-09-24_tornect_slice_g_closure.md`. Each fix below was written test first: the
test failed on the code before it and passes after (for G2-2 through a seam that first kept the old behaviour).

| Check | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| **G1-2 (LOW)**: a failed write of the portal restore token logs the error's kind, never its text, which fs-err fills with the token file's path | VERIFIED (unit) 2026-09-24 | `wayland::portal::tests::no_log_line_names_the_restore_token_file`, extended with a token path that is a directory and one whose directory would lie under a regular file: on the old code both new warnings named the temporary directory ("failed to open file `…/a-directory`: Is a directory"); now five lines, none with the path (`717d77a`). Closure round 1: VERIFIED CLOSED |
| **G1-1 (LOW)**: a session that ends before its source's blocking open has started still ends that open, so no desktop dialog stays up and no grant is kept for it | VERIFIED (unit) 2026-09-24; GNOME's dialog UNVERIFIED (as Q3) | Each open gets its own cancel, a `watch` made before `spawn_blocking` and latched `true` when the session ends or the stream's task is dropped; `MediaBackend::cancel_open` and the shared `opening` slot are gone (`79a06b1`). `tests::a_session_that_ends_before_its_open_starts_still_ends_it` (a double shaped like the old `SystemBackend`, held at a gate before it stores its cancel: on the old code the open ran on for 10 s, `cancelled: false`) and `tests::a_stream_dropped_while_its_source_opens_ends_the_open`. The gnullvm clippy is clean for the changed `#[cfg(windows)]` arm. Closure round 1: VERIFIED CLOSED |
| **G1-3 (LOW)**: a key-up or button-up the injector refuses (the Wayland portal's full queue) is owed and sent again, before the next event and by every release of everything | VERIFIED (unit) 2026-09-24 | `tests::a_release_the_sink_refused_is_sent_again` (`tornect-input`: the old `Tracked` had already struck the key, so nothing ever sent the key-up) (`996e6db`). `tests::release_all_sends_what_is_owed_and_owes_what_it_cannot_send` pins the direct `release_all()` path that the quiet release and the drop take; removing its retry, or its owing of a refusal, fails it (`fd7e2f7`). Closure round 1: VERIFIED CLOSED; round 2: `fd7e2f7` sound |
| **N-2 of the slice-G closure (INFO)**: owed releases go out in the order they were refused, so a Ctrl-drag that ends during a portal stall still drops with Ctrl down | VERIFIED (unit) 2026-09-24 | One owed list in refusal order replaces two sets that sent keys before buttons (`339f785`). `tests::owed_releases_go_out_in_the_order_they_were_refused`: on the old code Ctrl-up went out before the button-up |
| **G2-1 (LOW)**: the quiet release (3 s, C1-3) also comes while the host is stuck writing a picture or a `Pong` to a viewer that vanished | VERIFIED (unit) 2026-09-24; over real Tor UNVERIFIED | The reader stamps each frame as it reads it; the picture and `Pong` writes race the quiet deadline, let go of what the viewer holds when it passes, and go on: only the session's end cuts a write short (`4f12573`). Over a pipe that holds 16 bytes toward the viewer: `tests::a_key_held_by_a_viewer_that_vanishes_mid_picture_is_released` (on the old code "the held key was never released" within 10 s); the guard `tests::a_viewer_heard_while_a_picture_write_waits_keeps_its_key` (a ping every 100 ms at a quiet time of 300 ms: never released); `tests::a_viewer_whose_frames_fill_the_queue_is_let_go_one_quiet_period_later` (frames that fill the 64-deep queue count as heard for one more quiet period after the last one read, and no longer; both an unbounded rule and no rule fail it). A 1 KiB pipe let the old code pass, since the test card's pictures did not block the write in time. Recipe over Tor: an X11 host playing video, a held key, the viewer's network cut; time the release (≤ 3 s, up to about 30 s before). Closure round 2: VERIFIED CLOSED |
| **G1-4 (LOW)**: after a kill or a session's end, the portal thread makes nothing the viewer queued, and lets go of what its own calls hold down | VERIFIED (unit) 2026-09-24; on GNOME UNVERIFIED | The input loop moved unchanged into `pass_on` (`602d0f9`). Dropping the last handle marks the portal closing before the queue ends; the thread then discards what is queued and, when the queue ends, releases the keys and buttons its calls pressed (a call that timed out counts as made), and closes the session (`117fb34`). `wayland::tests::a_kill_in_a_stall_still_lets_go_of_what_the_portal_holds`: a real `WaylandInjector` under `Tracked` over a two-slot queue, the thread stalled on a move, the queue full when the injector drops, so its own key-up is refused: on the old loop both queued moves were made after the kill and no key-up at all; now `[A↓, move, A↑]`. Not relied on: that GNOME lets go of held keys on `Session.Close` (UNVERIFIED). Closure round 2: VERIFIED CLOSED, and the refactor `602d0f9` sound |
| **G2-2 (LOW)**: XTEST maps each pointer move over the root's size at that move, so a RandR change during a session keeps clicks where the viewer aims | VERIFIED (unit, a fake server) 2026-09-24; the unchanged root under Xvfb; NEEDS-DEVICE(x11) for a live resize | The injection runs over a small server seam, and the real one asks `GetGeometry` of the root at every move, as capture does per picture (`3379655`). `x11::tests::the_pointer_follows_a_resized_root` runs in the gate: on a root resized from 1280×720 to 1000×600, (32768, 32768) lands at (500, 300), where the size read at connect put it at (640, 360); a 1000×600 crop on a root shrunk to 800×500 lands (65535, 65535) at (799, 499). Under `xvfb-run -a -s "-screen 0 1280x720x24" env XDG_SESSION_TYPE=x11 cargo test --workspace -- x11::tests` both X11 input tests and the five X11 capture tests pass. This VM's Xvfb cannot resize its root, so the live check is for an X11 desktop: connect with control, change the resolution with `xrandr`, click at the picture's centre. Closure round 2: VERIFIED CLOSED in code; the live RandR change NEEDS-DEVICE(x11) |
| **G2-3 (INFO)**: 3 s of silence is inside Tor's delay tail, so a live viewer's drag can be let go of early | Disclosed (the notes' *Known limitations*); how often: UNVERIFIED | The trade-off against C1-3 and G2-1. With G2-1's rule, a viewer whose frames fill the queue while the host's write is stuck is let go of after two quiet periods (6 s). How often onion circuits stall for 3 s or more needs a live measurement |
| The window's code question outlives the host's wait, a late "the codes match" still pairs, and every end of the question is said in the window's terms (the fifth console check: the card ran out unseen after 50 s, the host kept the device, the window pinned nothing and said "run the command again") | VERIFIED (unit, and the real FSM over a pipe) 2026-09-24; live UNVERIFIED | The question stays up 10 min (`CONFIRM_LIMIT`): during the host's 50 s (`CONFIRM_TIMEOUT`) the card says how long the session request still waits, then that a yes still pairs this computer and that Connect asks for the session again; the accept stays pointer-only and armed (E1-1) (`a4887c2`). `ui::consent::tests::the_code_question_takes_a_late_answer` (a yes at 55 s; the old question had closed at 50 s), `ui::view::tests::the_code_card_says_a_late_yes_still_pairs`, and `ui::backend::tests::an_unconfirmed_pairing_ends_in_the_windows_words` (the real FSM against `serve_host`: a no, no answer, a yes at 70 s; the old window said "Nothing was pinned; run the command again to retry.: aborted: pairing not confirmed"; now the window's words, and the late yes leaves the host pinned). The command line keeps its 50 s. A retry from the half-paired state (the host pinned, the client not) pairs again through the host's "ALREADY PAIRED" card (M1-32; `fsm::tests::pairing_lost_is_reported_and_a_pinned_device_can_re_pair`). Closure round 2: sound on the security side, with one Info, R2-I1: after a late yes every error but a refused session read "Paired", also a known-hosts file that could not be written and a violation from a host still there; now only the host going away does (the stream closed, an I/O or framing failure, its goodbye), `ui::backend::tests::only_the_host_going_away_after_a_late_yes_reads_as_paired`, failing first (`a024c32`; closure round 3: VERIFIED CLOSED). The choice is decision M1-55 |
| A device the host paired that says nothing more until the host's 60 s wait ends is reported as paired (`PairedOnly`), as one that leaves is | VERIFIED (unit) 2026-09-24 | One pattern joins `Closed` and `PeerBye` in the arm after the post-accept `recv_expect` in `serve_host` (`crates/tornect-signal/src/fsm/host.rs:883-889`): `HandshakeError::Timeout(_)` (`f4bced7`). Only the reported outcome changes: the accept above it is the trust decision, nothing is sent either way, and no session is granted. `fsm::tests::a_device_the_host_paired_that_then_says_nothing_is_reported_paired`: on the old FSM `Err(Timeout("SESSION_REQUEST"))`, now `PairedOnly` after 60 s, the device pinned on the host and not on the client, no session popup and nothing accepted; a timeout before the accept (silence after `HELLO_ACK`, a pairing card nobody answers) still pins and grants nothing. The host's line now reads "paired with "…"; it asked for no session". Closure round 2: the line read line by line, an outcome label only |

## Home and the address book (M-UI-2 of the redesign; 2026-09-27)

Home's QR code of the ID, the name others see, the saved computers and the recent connections; the Address book
page (Computers, Recent, Devices that can connect here); the address book and history stores
(`crates/tornect-core/src/address_book.rs`); the preview without this machine's state; the open questions above the
page's scroll area. The files, all `0600` and replaced atomically, in the state directory next to
`known-hosts.toml`: **`address-book.toml`** (schema `version = 1`: per computer the host key its saved name was
given against, the saved name, the name it declared, the first and last day it was seen, a `later` table whose only
allowed key is the device words; and the history switch; no secret, no credential and no keychain entry name) and
**`recent.toml`** (`version = 1`: one line per computer and day). `known-hosts.toml` stays the pin store the pairing
code writes; the address book only names what it pins. Screenshots: `target/tornect-test/ui-m2/` (Home and the
address book, empty and populated, the QR code, the Recent and Devices tabs, both themes: 14).

| Check | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|

Both writers of a last-seen time follow the switch: the client handshake's `KnownHostStore::touch` (through
`apps/tornect/src/history.rs` `Pins`) and the host handshake's `TrustedDeviceStore::touch` (`DevicePins`; since
M-FEAT-1 a shared store that writes a pairing, an unpairing and a reject count the moment they happen, and a last-seen
time only with history on). With history on, those two files keep the handshake's times in seconds, as
before this milestone; the new files keep days. The session log is kept by design and is not covered by the switch.

The gate is green at **691 passed, 0 failed, 5 ignored** at `1457148` (2026-09-27), each commit of M-UI-2
gate-green on its own tree before it was committed.

## Device words (M-FEAT-1; 2026-09-28)

frozen parameters over a 32-byte key, role `onion` or `device`; four BIP39-English words from the first 44 bits of
`h`; the identicon from `h`; the pacer; the look-alike alarm), `apps/tornect/src/ui/recognize.rs` (what the sheets,
Home and the address book show, and the alarm's saved entries), and the host sheets, Home's ID card, the address

`target/tornect-test/feat1/`. Also in this range, the fixes of the M-UI-2 findings F-1, F-2, F-4 (security)
and P-L1 (privacy).

## Session levels (M-FEAT-2; 2026-09-28)

The levels of `docs/PLAN.md` §B.2.1 underneath the sheet (decisions M1-64, M1-65, M1-57): the wire keys
(§B.8.4.1), the level types and the availability table (`crates/tornect-core/src/levels.rs`), the probe
(`crates/tornect-platform/src/levels.rs`, the Windows reads in `win.rs`), the Linux polkit revocation
(`crates/tornect-platform/src/polkit.rs`, wired in `apps/tornect/src/host.rs`), the host FSM's check of the
answer (`fsm/host.rs`), and the existing sheet showing only what a row offers, the Tornect confirmation and the
client's stream bar (`apps/tornect/src/ui/`). The four-answer layout is M-UI-3's; the administrator helper and the
Windows prompt path are M-FEAT-3's. Screenshots: `target/tornect-test/feat2/`.

## History off by default (the operator's ruling of 2026-09-28)

"Off by default (Recommended)" — "Nothing about past connections is written unless you switch history on in Settings;
the first run says so in one line." The switch is the address book's `history` (M-UI-2); the first-run line on Home is
M-UI-3's. Superseded in its wording on 2026-09-29 (F4-M1): the switch covers the recent list only, and the session log
records requests to this computer, and this computer's unattended connections to others, whatever it says.

| Check | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| A new Tornect home keeps no history: a visit writes no file, and a stored value (on or off) is kept by every later load | VERIFIED (unit) | `address_book::tests::history_is_off_until_switched_on`; `a_book_round_trips_through_a_private_file` (a missing file is a book with history off) |
| On a fresh home, where the switch was never touched, neither a connection (client) nor a session request (host) writes anything about past connections: the pins are written at their pairing time, with no visit, and after that no file changes | VERIFIED (unit) | `history::tests::a_fresh_home_writes_no_history` (bytes, modification times and the directory, both sides) |
| The switch's text when off says what switching it on keeps, and what the switch does not cover | VERIFIED (unit) | `ui/pages/address_book.rs::HISTORY_OFF` (F4-M1, FR2-I1): "Off: no list of recent connections is kept. Switched on, it keeps the day of each connection (never the time) and each computer's last-connected day, only on this computer; anything kept before stays until you clear it. The switch does not cover the session log: it always records requests to this computer, and your unattended connections to others, with their time, on this computer only."; `pages::tests::the_history_texts_name_the_session_log` |

(`apps/tornect/src/ui/`, `apps/tornect/src/{host,prompt}.rs`, `crates/tornect-core/src/{levels,words,telemetry}.rs`,
`crates/tornect-platform/src/{polkit,levels,win}.rs`). Every existing test passes unchanged. Screenshots:
`target/tornect-test/ui-m3/`.

## The Windows administrator helper (M-FEAT-3; 2026-09-29)

`tornect input-helper`, the only elevated part of Tornect, and everything around it (`docs/PLAN.md` §B.2.1,
§B.8.7.5, §B.8.8, §B.8.13; decisions M1-57, M1-62): the frame protocol and the helper's loop
(`crates/tornect-input/src/helper.rs`), the pipe, the launch and the checks (`crates/tornect-platform/src/helper.rs`,
`win_helper.rs`), the on-demand task (`helper_task.rs`, `win_task.rs`), the FSM seam between the answer and the
accept (`crates/tornect-signal/src/fsm/host.rs`), the host's side (`apps/tornect/src/admin_helper.rs`), the command
(`helper_cmd.rs`), the secure-screen watch (`secure_screen.rs`), and the carried items of step 0. **Every Windows call is
type-checked only on the build VM** (no Windows machine); every decision is a pure function tested here, the channel
end to end over a Unix socket pair standing in for the named pipe.

**`ADMIN_HELPER` stays `false` in this build:** switching it on makes the installed row offer "Allow as administrator…"
and the no-prompt row its answer, and two existing sheet tests of M-FEAT-2 pin the build without the helper
(`view::tests::the_session_sheet_offers_only_the_levels_of_its_row`: `!prompt.has("Allow as administrator…")`;
`consent::tests::a_session_sheet_carries_its_levels_and_drops_them`: `SheetLevels::of(&prompt).answers.admin == None`).
The brief keeps trust-surface tests unchanged and says to stop and report instead; with the flag set on the tree of
`19cb0de`, every other test passes (`cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast`: 810 passed, 2 failed — those two —
6 ignored; `target/tmp/feat3-flip-tests-kept.log`). Until the flag is on, the sheet hides the answer and says why, and
the helper path of the FSM is unreachable.

| Check | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|

| The host-side level is the signed level (H5; M-UI-3 Security F-1): `LiveSession` carries the elevation of `SESSION_ACCEPT`; the banner, the indicator and the terminal read it there | VERIFIED (unit) | `fsm::tests::the_host_side_level_is_the_signed_one`; `backend::tests::the_banner_and_indicator_read_the_signed_level` |
| `sessions.log`: `session_started` carries `elevation` (`admin`, absent at none, so older lines read the same; M-UI-3 Security F-16); `admin_helper_started`, `admin_helper_stopped`, `admin_helper_failed` with a fixed reason | VERIFIED (unit) | `session_log::tests::the_level_and_the_helper_events_reach_the_log` (core); `fsm::tests::the_accept_carries_the_administrator_level_only_with_the_helper_attached` (the FSM's Started and Failed; runs with the flag on); `admin_helper::tests::the_helpers_panic_chord_fires_the_kill_path` (Stopped) |
| H1: the helper accepts only the input events in their one canonical encoding, `ReleaseAll`, `Stop` and `Heartbeat`; any other 16 bytes (every kind byte, every bit of every field) or a frame cut short ends it | VERIFIED (unit) | `helper::tests::{every_frame_kind_round_trips, a_foreign_byte_is_refused, every_exit_lets_go_first}` (tornect-input) |

| H2: `input-helper` runs before the parser (`--home` reads `TORNECT_HOME`), the log and the paths, after the DLL search is restricted to System32; it reads its three arguments only; a malformed command line or a missing pipe ends it at once with its own exit code | VERIFIED (unit) for the command; the dispatch order by reading `main.rs`; the loader's use of the image's dependent-load flag NEEDS-DEVICE(windows) (the E2-4 row above) | `helper_cmd::tests::the_helper_ends_at_once_without_its_pipe`; `helper::tests::the_command_line_is_exactly_pipe_pid_and_limit` (platform) |
| H4: the pipe's name holds 128 random bits; its DACL is protected and names this user, plus Administrators on a standard account, never the default descriptor; one instance created first (`FILE_FLAG_FIRST_PIPE_INSTANCE`: a pipe that exists fails the creation, "administrator access unavailable"); remote clients refused; the helper opens it for identification only | VERIFIED (unit) for the name and the DACL string; the calls NEEDS-DEVICE(windows) | `helper::tests::{a_pipe_name_has_128_random_bits_and_nothing_else_passes, the_pipe_admits_this_user_and_on_a_standard_account_administrators}` |
| The checks each end makes (amendment §1.3 (d), (e); M-FEAT-2 Security F-10): the helper checks the server's process id, the same executable path and the very same file (volume and file index), its own location under Program Files as its open file resolves it, and the same session; the host checks the client Windows started (attended) or the registered image, elevated, in its session (task); a fact not read refuses | VERIFIED (unit) for the decisions; the reads NEEDS-DEVICE(windows) | `helper::tests::{the_helper_refuses_a_wrong_server, the_host_refuses_a_wrong_client}` |

| H6, H7: the task `\Tornect admin helper <SID>` exists only while an active admin grant does, disabled while unattended access is off; registered from Program Files only (never a `%` path), read back and compared (principal, run level, command, arguments, one action, no trigger), a different one removed; the folder and executable ACL checked at registration and before each run | VERIFIED (unit, a fake scheduler); nothing registers it in this build (admin grants are M-FEAT-5) | `helper_task::tests::{the_task_lives_exactly_while_an_admin_grant_does, the_registration_is_read_back_and_a_different_one_removed, a_percent_path_or_one_outside_program_files_is_refused, a_folder_others_can_change_refuses_registration_and_run, the_readiness_line_says_whether_it_is_registered}` |

**The Windows recipe (NEEDS-DEVICE(windows), the operator's PC, after the flag is on):** install to `C:\Program Files\Tornect`;
(1) an administrator account, UAC at its default: a second device asks for a session, "Allow as administrator…",
Windows' consent prompt on the secure desktop; Yes: the session starts, the banner says "as administrator", the
viewer's clicks reach an elevated `regedit` or an elevated `cmd`; `sessions.log` has `admin_helper_started` and
`session_started` with `"elevation":"admin"`; Task Manager shows one `tornect.exe` running elevated for the session only;
(2) the same, No at Windows' prompt: the sheet comes back with "Administrator access was not given", the other answers,
armed after a second; the popup's time keeps running; (3) a standard account: Windows asks for an administrator's name
and password; the session's input reaches elevated windows; (4) panic key, the window's Stop and `tornect panic` with an
elevated window focused: the session ends and the elevated `tornect.exe` is gone within 2 s; (5) kill the host from
Task Manager during an administrator session: the helper exits (pipe closed or host exit); (6) with a secure screen
(Ctrl+Alt+Del, a UAC prompt) during a Control session: the window's events say the notice; (7) `PromptOnSecureDesktop` 0:
the sheet says Windows' prompts appear on the shared screen, and whether the viewer can answer a prompt drawn there
(if yes, those machines join the UAC-off row); (8) an older version's elevated start-at-login task, then
`tornect service install`: Windows' prompt removes it, `schtasks /Query /TN Tornect /XML` shows `LeastPrivilege`;
(9) Windows 11's administrator protection, if the PC has it: whether the helper's checks still pass;
(10) once admin grants exist (M-FEAT-5): the task appears with the first admin grant, disappears with the last, is
disabled while unattended access is off, and an unattended administrator session starts through it, or at none with
"administrator access unavailable" when it does not connect within 5 s.

The gate is green at **814 passed, 0 failed, 6 ignored** at `4699f2a` (2026-09-29), each commit of M-FEAT-3
gate-green on its own tree before it was committed. Two hardening commits after the screenshots: the helper pipe's
writes wait at most 2 s (`af3c668`), and the helper's threads have explicit stack sizes, so the standard library reads
no `RUST_MIN_STACK` in the elevated process (`4d5878e`).

## Before the tag: the carried findings of M-UI-3 and M-FEAT-3 (2026-09-29)

One question at a time on the request sheet, and the carried findings of M-UI-3 (U3-M1, U3-L2, U3-L3, F-3 of its
trust surfaces, the scam window) and M-FEAT-3 (F-1, F-2, F-3, F-4, F-8), each in its own commit, each gate-green on its
own tree (`apps/tornect/src/{admin_helper,host,main}.rs`, `apps/tornect/src/ui/{backend,recognize,view}.rs`,
`apps/tornect/src/ui/pages/mod.rs`, `crates/tornect-core/src/{telemetry,words}.rs`,
`crates/tornect-platform/src/{autostart,helper,helper_task,win,win_task}.rs`). `ADMIN_HELPER` stays false. Existing
tests changed, each named in its row: `view::tests::a_card_that_moves_waits_another_second` (the one trust-surface
test the new sheet changes), `pages::tests::the_newest_questions_answers_stay_in_view` (it needed two cards on screen),
`words::tests::labels_that_would_put_words_in_the_name_slot_are_refused` (two labels it passed are now refused),
`recognize::tests::paired_moments_before_is_ten_minutes` (renamed `paired_moments_before_is_a_day`),
`autostart::tests::only_an_administrator_account_passes_the_elevated_check` (the reworded message), the fixtures of
`helper_task::tests` (each entry gains its flags; the fake reads an owner) and of `admin_helper::tests` (the fake pending
helper beats from its `Ready`; the fake helper closes its end when it ends), and the literal of
`telemetry::tests::init_twice_does_not_panic` (the new option). Screenshots: `target/tornect-test/fix1/`.

gate-green on its own tree before it was committed; the Windows target type-checks (`cargo clippy --target
x86_64-pc-windows-gnullvm --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, rc 0) at `d9cc0f0`, `0bc2fc8` and `eef27e8`.

## The unattended client and saved credentials (M-FEAT-4; 2026-09-29)

The client side of §B.8.10 and §B.8.9.1, in the window and on the command line, each commit gate-green on its own tree
(`crates/tornect-core/src/{words,session_log}.rs`, `crates/tornect-core/src/unattended/{credentials,hosts}.rs`,
`crates/tornect-signal/src/fsm/{client,mod,tests}.rs`, `apps/tornect/src/{main,tor_cmd,ua_client,unattended,host}.rs`,
`apps/tornect/src/ui/{app,backend,book,model,preview,view}.rs`, `apps/tornect/src/ui/pages/{home,address_book,mod}.rs`,
the manifests). No wire change; the host side's FSM, sheets and their tests are untouched. Existing tests changed, each
named in its row: the label-refusal texts of `words::tests` and `view::tests::a_name_that_reads_like_words_is_not_saved`
(the plainer sentence), `pages::tests::home_connects_to_a_saved_computer_and_shows_the_qr_code` (the saved cards read
Knock), `pages::tests::every_sheet_draws_and_keys_only_refuse` (extended), and the literals that gained a field
(`Viewing.unattended`, `Dial.unattended`) in `view::tests`, `backend::tests`, `pages::tests` and the preview.
Screenshots: `target/tornect-test/feat4/`.

## Before the tag: the fix round 2 (2026-10-01)

## Unattended host completeness (M-FEAT-5; 2026-10-02)

Decision M1-67 (`docs/PLAN.md` row 7), each commit gate-green on its own tree. `ADMIN_HELPER` stays false; no manifest
change. **The client's rule** (`crates/tornect-core/src/unattended/{credentials,hosts}.rs`,
`crates/tornect-signal/src/fsm/{mod,client,tests}.rs`, `apps/tornect/src/{ua_client,tor_cmd,host}.rs`,
`apps/tornect/src/ui/backend.rs`): the client decides about a remembered password from the host's verified answer
alone, before QUIC. Until the wire carries `grant_reach`, every unattended answer reads as one that leaves the key
out: nothing is kept, and a remembered password is deleted at the answer. Existing tests changed, each named in its
row: two rewritten for the new rule (`unattended::credentials::tests::{remember_is_offered_only_with_a_keychain_and_never_for_administrator_without_a_gate, an_administrator_grant_keeps_no_password_without_a_presence_gate}`),
one assertion added to `fsm::tests::an_unattended_accept_above_the_request_is_a_violation`, and call shapes only
(`Attempt::Accepted { elevation, grant_reach }`, `settle`'s new argument and result) in
`unattended::credentials::tests::{a_password_is_kept_after_an_accept_and_stale_only_when_refused, forget_deletes_the_entry_and_the_flag}`,
`ua_client::tests::{a_failed_save_leaves_no_password_behind, forget_deletes_the_password_when_the_file_cannot_be_read, rotate_id_deletes_every_hosts_password_whatever_its_flag, a_remembered_password_that_vanished_is_asked_for_again, only_a_refusal_of_the_remembered_password_deletes_it, unreachable_wrong_password_and_a_broken_stream_read_differently}`
and the trust-surface test `host::tests::a_password_remembered_after_an_unattended_connect_is_in_no_file` (its
hand-built accept passes `grant_reach: Some(0)` and reads `settle`'s `.remembered`; every assertion unchanged).

|---|---|---|

| D3, D4, D9, ND-2, DA-18 (the raise rules): a grant's own reach rises (the level, or control where control reaches the level through clause (ii)) only together with a new generated password in the same write. `set_options` refuses it (`G_NEEDS_PASSWORD` = 0x0108, logged, nothing changed); a set-password (`RegFinish` for an existing grant, the window's `set_password`, which now takes options) may carry exactly that raise, and only with `generated`; a creation with reach without `generated` is refused, decided again under the lock of the commit; a `RegFinish` whose `generated` differs from its `RegStart`'s, or that raises after a `RegStart` that did not declare a raise, is refused; *enable* refuses a marked grant at the level whatever disabled it; every registration sets `unmarked` exactly when the password was sent as generated. The IPC carries `RegStart{raise, generated}`, `RegFinish{generated}` and `GrantOptions.elevation`; the command line sends `false` for both until its administrator forms, and the window has no caller, so nothing in this build creates or raises an admin grant. | D6, A12, A25 (p) (the per-device end): beside the host-wide kill switch, `KillSwitch::end_device` ends sessions of one device, each told why (`BYE{REVOKED}` for a revoke, disable, unpair or lowering; `BYE{SUSPENDED}` for a local or remote suspend or a Block; `BYE{PANIC}` for the panic key's suspend), and leaves every other session running. An unattended session watches for its device from its reservation, so an end committed after the read that follows the reservation ends the session at once when it starts; a live one ends within the second. `EndScope::Every` (a revoke, disable, unpair or suspend, local or remote) ends every session of the device; `EndScope::AtTheLevel` (a lowering, by `set_options` or with a new password, judged under the lock that commits it) only those whose accept put them at the level, so a session below the level goes on. Fired by the guard through `DeviceSessionEnd` (wired to the host's kill switch; the offline route has none) once its write committed, for the owner's own changes and for a remote revoke or suspend alike, so the end never waits on the requester's answer; the state machine ends the target again after that answer, for an admin authority without that wiring. | §B.8.7.4, the Windows control channel (M-FEAT-5 builds the pipe; until now every Windows host had none): `\\.\pipe\tornect-ctl-<32 hex digits>`, a name drawn from 128 random bits at each host start, never computed from the state directory (another account can neither predict it nor create the pipe first, and no part of the path is in the name); once the pipe's first instance exists the host writes the name into `<state_dir>\ipc.pipe` (a new temporary file made with the pipe's descriptor, written, flushed and moved over the name file); a host whose name file cannot be written has no channel and removes an earlier name file, best effort; the endpoint removes the name file when it is dropped, if the file still holds its name; the command line takes the name from that file only (at most 128 bytes read): no file, or no pipe of that name, is no host, and a file that holds anything but exactly such a name is refused before any pipe is opened ("the control channel's name file is not one Tornect wrote; no host was asked"); an explicit, protected DACL that admits this account alone (`D:P(A;;GA;;;<SID>)`), never the default descriptor, on the pipe and on the name file; the first instance created with `FILE_FLAG_FIRST_PIPE_INSTANCE`, so a pipe another process made first fails the creation and is never used (`IpcError::NameTaken`: no channel, the host says Ctrl-C ends its sessions); `PIPE_REJECT_REMOTE_CLIENTS`; the host checks the client's account (its SID, read from its process through `GetNamedPipeClientProcessId`) before it reads a frame, and the client checks the server's (`GetNamedPipeServerProcessId`) before it sends anything, an account that cannot be read refused; the client opens the pipe for identification only; the same framing as the Unix socket (one shared loop). Up to `MAX_CONNECTIONS` connections at once, as on Unix (track D S6: `off`, `revoke` and `panic` never wait behind another request's dialog): every instance is created by the host, the next one before a connected one is handed over, so the name never lapses; a client finding every instance busy waits up to 30 s | VERIFIED (unit) for the rules that need no Windows; the calls type-checked (`cargo clippy --target x86_64-pc-windows-gnullvm`) and NEEDS-DEVICE(windows) | `ipc::pipe_rules::{a_second_server_instance_fails_to_create_and_serves_nothing, the_client_refuses_a_server_whose_sid_differs, a_control_pipe_name_is_random_each_time, the_name_file_round_trips, no_name_file_means_no_host, a_name_file_that_is_not_a_control_pipe_is_refused}` (platform; every system); the Unix endpoint's tests unchanged and green on the shared framing. Recipe: on Windows, (1) start `tornect host`, then `tornect unattended status` and `tornect panic` from a second terminal: both answer; (2) `Get-Acl \\.\pipe\tornect-ctl-*` or Sysinternals `accesschk -p`: one ACE, this account, full control, protected; `icacls %LOCALAPPDATA%\Tornect\ipc.pipe`: this account only; (3) a second `tornect host` for the same home: refused by the host lock; a second account cannot read the name file and cannot predict the name: from it, `type %LOCALAPPDATA%\Tornect\ipc.pipe` (the first account's folder) is refused, and stopping and starting the host shows another `tornect-ctl-` name each time in `[System.IO.Directory]::GetFiles("\\.\pipe\")`; (4) from another account on the same machine: the pipe cannot be opened; (5) from another machine (`\\<host>\pipe\…`): refused; (6) a server of another account at a name written into the name file by hand (with a permissive DACL): the CLI refuses it before sending; (7) with the host running, its name file edited to hold any other text: `tornect panic` says the host has no control channel and opens no pipe |

| `tornect unattended set-level <device> none` (PLAN:1475, 1495-1496, 1529, 1538; decision M1-68), built in M-FEAT-6: `IpcOp::SetLevelNone` (tag 22, the device only) hands the guard's `set_options` the level and nothing else (every other field `None` keeps the grant's; nothing read from the store fills them, so a change made meanwhile is kept and the widening branch is never reached). Deny-direction: no re-authentication, also during a remote session; a lowering ends that device's sessions at the administrator level (D6), a session below the level goes on; `GrantChanged` logged. A grant without the level is left as it is and the command says "<device> has no administrator access; nothing changed."; on Linux, where no grant holds the level, that is the line printed, not PLAN:1496's Linux refusal (for the operator to rule). After a lowering the command prints the turn-off sentence (PLAN:1419-1423) and, from the answer's payload, its condition: another grant holds the level, or the helper task stays registered (a presence that cannot be read counts). Only `none` parses. A host without its store refuses it (M1-46). The window has no caller yet (M-UI-5) | VERIFIED (unit); NEEDS-DEVICE(windows): `tornect unattended set-level <device> none` during an administrator session: the session ends and the sentence is printed (with `ADMIN_HELPER` false no administrator session can exist yet, so this waits for the device test) | `unattended::tests::{set_level_none_needs_no_reauth_and_lowers, set_level_none_sends_only_the_elevation, set_level_none_never_reaches_reauth, set_level_none_never_raises, set_level_none_ends_only_the_admin_session, set_level_none_on_a_grant_at_none_changes_nothing, a_storeless_host_refuses_set_level_none, set_level_parses_only_none, set_level_prints_the_turn_off_sentence, set_level_prints_the_turn_off_sentence_with_its_condition}` (the first, with the dispatch still refusing the op, failed with `G_UNSUPPORTED` on 2026-10-03); `unattended::ipc::tests::{set_level_none_roundtrips_with_its_tag, the_set_level_none_payload_roundtrips_and_bounds_keeps}` |
| B ("a grant lowered since the gate is clamped there"), D11 (the mode at the read after the reservation): the read returns the grant's mode as stored under its lock (`ReachRead::grant_mode`), and the hook lowers the session to it before the level and the served mode are decided, so clause (ii) and the mark are judged on the mode served: a grant narrowed to View only while its request waits (in its block window, say) is served View only, never control at `grant_reach` 0 | VERIFIED (unit; the real gate and FSMs over a pipe) | `unattended::tests::the_read_gives_the_grant_mode_as_stored`; `fsm::tests::a_grant_narrowed_in_the_block_window_is_served_view_only` (a password marked before, nothing at the level; and one this answer marks just after another device was given the level) |
| §B.8.6.2, §B.8.6.4 (no popup and no block window while another session is live): an unattended request that got through its PAKE while another remote session is live or reserved opens neither "Ask before starting" nor the block window (nor, on Linux, its notification), so no viewer of the live session sees the requester's label. After its post-F2 and readiness checks the gate asks the host's live-session view (`UnattendedContext::activity`), logs `popup_suppressed_remote_session` ("session") as the attended popup does, and answers `SESSION_ACTIVE` (a post-F2 refusal, before the popup in §B.8.4.5's `AwaitKe3` row, and the code the reservation gives it), whether or not the grant asks before starting | VERIFIED (unit; the real gate and FSMs over a pipe) | `fsm::tests::a_live_session_suppresses_the_block_window_and_the_ask` (no window and no popup opened, both log lines; with that session over, the same request opens them) |
| §B.8.7.2 (normalized on both sides): the command line prints a generated password as it is registered (NFC, one space between words: a list word's hyphen, as in "t-shirt", prints as a space), and the client normalizes the typed password the same way before every unattended PAKE (`fsm::client`, sessions and admin commands alike), so a phrase typed with the list's hyphen or a run of spaces still logs in | VERIFIED (unit) | `unattended::tests::a_generated_password_is_printed_as_registered_and_logs_in` (the printed text logs in against the stored record, another password does not); `fsm::tests::a_typed_password_is_normalized_before_the_pake` |
| D4 (the plain command-line forms): `grant` and `set-password` without `--administrator` register a chosen password, never as generated and never raising (`RegStart{raise: false, generated: false}`, `RegFinish{generated: false}`): their dialog is the plain one; a password change leaves the password marked, of a grant at none and of one at the level alike; a new Control grant where control reaches the level is refused `G_NEEDS_PASSWORD`, nothing written. The registration after the terminal's prompt is its own function (`register_with`), so a test drives it | VERIFIED (unit, the offline route) | `unattended::tests::the_plain_forms_send_a_chosen_password_and_no_raise` |

| `tornect install` and `tornect uninstall` on Windows (PLAN:274-282; decisions M1-57, M1-65, M1-69): the Program Files install as checked steps, over a recording fake of Windows in the tests and over Windows' own calls in the program. This program's image is opened with share mode read only and hashed (SHA-256) from that handle before the guard's dialog, held until the install ends and named by the held handle's final path; an image not named `tornect.exe`, or whose path holds `%`, `"` or a control character, is refused right after it is held. The folder is `FOLDERID_ProgramFiles`'s `Tornect`; an existing one is refused, before the dialog and again right after it, when it holds anything but `tornect.exe` ("<folder> already holds other files, so Tornect was not installed there."), when it or that `tornect.exe` is a reparse point, or when it, that `tornect.exe` or Program Files fails the ACL rule below; folder or no folder, the install is also refused while Windows is to remove the folder at the next restart (or cannot say) and over an earlier install ("Tornect is already installed in <folder>. Run tornect uninstall first."). One guard operation covers two prompts. The first runs the system folder's robocopy for the copy; after it the folder and the copy are checked for reparse points, the copy is hashed and compared with the held image ("Something else replaced the Tornect file while it was being copied, so nothing was installed.") and then checked with the administrator helper task's ACL rule (the executable, the folder and Program Files: owned by SYSTEM, Administrators or TrustedInstaller, with no write, delete or owner-change entry for anyone else; else "The installed copy could be changed by accounts other than administrators, so nothing points at it and Tornect was not installed. It was not deleted as administrator either, since another account could have changed what would be deleted: delete the Tornect folder in Program Files by hand."), all before anything points at it; then this user's Start-menu entry `Tornect.lnk`; then the second prompt runs the installed copy itself (`tornect install-entry`), which checks itself again and writes the Apps-and-features entry `Tornect` under HKLM (`DisplayName` "Tornect", `DisplayVersion` this build's version, no publisher, `UninstallString` the quoted path with the fixed argument `uninstall`; a path that cannot be quoted is refused before the dialog). Each elevated step runs only through its own program (the section "The Windows install in Program Files" below). A failure undoes what was done, newest first, the folder's elevated delete only after a reparse check of its own; a copy that failed the ACL check, or whose folder or file became a reparse point, is never deleted as administrator; the reparse test, the file read and the security read act on the path itself, never through a reparse point; a dismissed prompt says "Installing needs administrator approval; nothing was installed.". Uninstall targets the known folder only, checked (not a reparse point, administrators only) before the dialog and again before its elevated step, and removes the uninstall entry (reg), the Start-menu entry, then the folder (cmd); run from the installed copy, as Apps and features runs it, that copy marks itself and its folder for removal at the next restart instead (`tornect remove-at-restart`, waited for), and the command says so. It is refused while a grant holds the administrator level or the helper task is registered ("Remove administrator access first: tornect unattended set-level <device> none", with the devices) and while Tornect cannot read its grants; install refuses a custom home | VERIFIED (unit, the steps over a fake); the Windows calls type-checked only; NEEDS-DEVICE(windows): the recipes of the section "The Windows install in Program Files" below | `install::windows_tests::{the_source_is_held_and_hashed_before_the_reauth, program_files_comes_from_the_known_folder_never_the_environment, a_swapped_source_is_detected_and_nothing_points_at_it, a_non_empty_existing_folder_is_refused, a_write_entry_after_the_copy_undoes_the_install, the_uninstall_command_is_quoted_with_its_fixed_argument, an_unquotable_path_is_refused, a_cancelled_uac_leaves_nothing, uninstall_reverses_every_step, uninstall_targets_only_the_known_folder, uninstall_never_elevates_a_copy_outside_program_files, an_existing_folder_is_checked_before_the_dialog, a_folder_changed_during_the_dialog_is_refused_before_the_copy, the_copy_is_never_read_or_removed_through_a_link, an_install_never_deletes_as_administrator_a_folder_found_unsafe, a_copy_others_can_change_is_refused_and_nothing_points_at_it, the_acl_check_runs_after_the_copy_and_before_any_entry}` (tornect-platform; the third, with the steps refusing before they start, failed on 2026-10-03; the refusal after the copy and its place in the order are exercised by the last two, which failed on 2026-10-03 with the fake's ACL answers ignored; the four before them failed on 2026-10-03 against the steps without these checks); `install_cmd::tests::{install_refuses_a_custom_home_on_windows, uninstall_is_refused_while_an_admin_grant_exists, install_and_uninstall_are_refused_while_a_host_runs}`; and the tests of that section |
