## The Windows install in Program Files (2026-10-05)

The operator's rulings of 2026-10-03 ("Two UAC prompts") and 2026-10-05 (decision M1-69; `docs/PLAN.md` §B.1.1 and
the CLI list of §B.8.7.4), each commit gate-green on its own tree. `ADMIN_HELPER` stays false and nothing registers the
administrator helper task; no manifest change. The Windows calls are type-checked (`cargo clippy --workspace
--all-targets --target x86_64-pc-windows-gnullvm`); no test makes one: every test runs over the recording fake of
Windows or a fake of its own. The device checks run the cross-built `tornect.exe` (the release zip's file).

| Check | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Each elevated step runs only through its own program (decision M1-65): the copy through the system folder's `robocopy.exe` (`"<source folder>" "<Program Files>\Tornect" tornect.exe /R:0 /W:0 /COPY:DATX`: the file's data, attributes and times, never an alternate data stream; a success only with exit code 1; a source at a drive root written `"D:\."`), the uninstall entry's delete through its `reg.exe` (`delete "HKLM\Software\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Uninstall\Tornect" /f /reg:64`), the folder's removal through its `cmd.exe` (`/d /v:off /c rmdir /s /q "<folder>"`: no AutoRun command, no delayed expansion), the entry's write and the removal at the next restart through the installed copy itself (`install-entry`, `remove-at-restart`), only after its hash and ACL checks passed in this run. Any other program, another step's system program and PowerShell included, is refused before the dialog with nothing run: "Tornect runs nothing as administrator from <program>: this step runs only <its program>, which other accounts cannot change.". One guard operation covers both prompts. Robocopy keeps the file's name, so an image not named `tornect.exe` (any case) is refused right after it is held, before anything is asked ("Tornect can be installed only from a file named tornect.exe. Rename this copy to tornect.exe and run it again."), and so is one whose path holds `%`, `"` or a control character ("<path> holds a character Windows' copy cannot take (%, " or a control character), so Tornect was not installed from it.", the path in quotes with its backslashes doubled) | VERIFIED (unit) | `install::windows_tests::{each_elevated_step_runs_only_its_own_program, the_entry_is_written_only_by_the_installed_copy, the_installed_copy_runs_nothing_after_a_failed_acl_check, each_elevated_step_has_its_fixed_program_and_arguments, robocopy_succeeds_only_when_it_copied_the_file, a_source_not_named_tornect_exe_is_refused_before_anything_is_asked, one_guard_operation_covers_both_prompts}` (tornect-platform; all but the third and the last failed first on 2026-10-05, against two wrong placeholders and the old rule) |

## Before the tag: the control channel, the block window, the panic key and four corrections

The operator's rulings of 2026-10-03 and 2026-10-05 (decision M1-69; `docs/PLAN.md` §B.8.6.3, §B.8.7.4, §B.8.9.1 and
Part C row 9a), each commit gate-green on its own tree. `ADMIN_HELPER` stays false; the administrator helper's pipe and
the Unix socket are unchanged. The Windows calls are type-checked (`cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --target
x86_64-pc-windows-gnullvm`); no test makes one.

| Check | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| The Windows control pipe gets a fresh random name at each host start (§B.8.7.4): `\\.\pipe\tornect-ctl-` and the 32 lowercase hex digits of 16 random bytes, never the administrator helper's prefix; once the first instance exists the host writes the name into `<state_dir>\ipc.pipe` with the pipe's this-account-only descriptor (a new temporary file, written, flushed, then moved over the name file), and without that file it has no channel; the endpoint removes the file when dropped if it still holds its name; the command line takes the name from that file only: no file is no host, and anything but exactly such a name is refused before a pipe is opened; no code computes a name from the state directory any more | VERIFIED (unit) for the name, the file's write, read and removal; the calls type-checked; NEEDS-DEVICE(windows) | `ipc::pipe_rules::{a_control_pipe_name_is_random_each_time, the_name_file_round_trips, no_name_file_means_no_host, a_name_file_that_is_not_a_control_pipe_is_refused}` (platform; every system; failed first on 2026-10-05). Device: start `tornect host`, stop it, start it again: `[System.IO.Directory]::GetFiles("\\.\pipe\")` shows a `tornect-ctl-` name that differs at each start; `icacls %LOCALAPPDATA%\Tornect\ipc.pipe` shows this account only; from a second account the file cannot be read; `tornect panic` and `tornect unattended status` answer; once the host has stopped, the file is gone |
| A name file left by a host that did not stop cleanly is trusted only for a pipe of this account (§B.8.7.4): the command line counts the pipe the name file names as a running host only once it opened it and the account of its server, read from the server's process id, is this account, the check a request makes before anything is sent; a pipe that another account made since under that name, or one whose server's account or process cannot be read, is no host, so the command line falls to the host lock, and to the offline guard when no host holds it; a pipe whose every instance is busy still counts as a host | VERIFIED (unit) for the decision; the calls type-checked; NEEDS-DEVICE(windows) | `ipc::pipe_rules::a_pipe_of_another_account_is_no_running_host` (platform; every system). Device: `taskkill /F` the host, create its dead `tornect-ctl-` pipe from a second account, run `tornect unattended off`: it must fall to the offline guard |
| A request whose block window could not be shown is refused (§B.8.6.3): the window host's block window counts as shown only once one of its windows drew its first frame (reported where the app paints the block windows; a window created but never drawn counts for nothing) or, on Linux, once the notification server showed its notification; with neither before the window's end, or as soon as every window failed to open and no notification is up or coming, the request is refused `HOST_NOT_READY` without a `cause` before any accept, one `HostNotReady` line names the device, and the grant is left as it was (nothing is suspended, unlike a Block); a Block at the same moment still blocks; the terminal host's rule is unchanged (a block line it cannot write, or a closed terminal, refuses as a Block) | VERIFIED (unit); NEEDS-DEVICE(windows), NEEDS-DEVICE(gnome) | `ui::block::tests::a_block_window_shown_nowhere_refuses_the_request`, `ui::app::tests::a_block_surface_that_fails_reports_it` (apps/tornect), `fsm::tests::a_block_window_not_shown_is_refused_and_the_grant_stays` (tornect-signal; all failed first on 2026-10-05); for "a window created but never drawn counts for nothing", `ui::app::tests::a_block_window_that_opened_is_not_shown_until_it_draws` (apps/tornect; the windows that opened are kept through the same code as the app's GPU windows, which reports nothing for them; failed first on 2026-10-07 with a report of a drawn frame added where an opened window is kept). Device, on Windows and on Linux: with an unattended grant whose block window is 5 s, make the block window fail to open (on Linux a session whose compositor refuses new windows, with no notification daemon running; on Windows a window host whose GPU surface cannot be created) and connect from the other device: the client is refused as not ready, `tornect unattended status` shows the grant still active, and `tornect unattended log` has a `host_not_ready` line for that device; with the windows working, the same connect shows the block window and starts after 5 s |
| Every operation on `hosts.toml` (the remembered passwords' records and the pin of each host's key) holds, beside this process's mutex, an OS lock on `hosts.toml.lock` next to it (§B.8.9.1): created this account's only if missing, taken exclusively, tried every 50 ms for at most 10 s, released when the operation ends; so the window and a command line never save over each other. A lock not taken in time fails the operation with "another Tornect held the lock beside hosts.toml for more than 10 s; nothing was saved", and one that cannot be opened or taken at all with "the lock beside hosts.toml could not be taken (<why>); nothing was saved": nothing saves without it. The pin check then answers as for an unreadable file, a mismatch (no proof is sent, never "no pin yet", which would trust a new host key), and the pin's record writes nothing; Forget, Rotate ID and the settling of an answer still delete a remembered password first, as they do when `hosts.toml` cannot be read. The lock orders the saves, not the answers | VERIFIED (unit); NEEDS-DEVICE(windows) for the Windows lock | `ua_client::tests::{a_second_holder_waits_for_the_hosts_file_lock, a_lock_that_cannot_be_taken_fails_the_save, a_pin_check_without_the_lock_is_a_mismatch}` (apps/tornect; failed first on 2026-10-05). Device (Windows): with the window open, run `tornect connect <id> --unattended` for a host whose password is remembered while the window connects to another such host: `hosts.toml` afterwards holds both records; while another program holds `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Tornect\hosts.toml.lock` open with an exclusive lock (PowerShell `[System.IO.File]::Open(path, 'OpenOrCreate', 'ReadWrite', 'None')`), a connect says after 10 s that another Tornect held the lock and saves nothing |
| The panic key's suspend (§B.8.7.4, Part C row 9a): after the host-wide kill of the panic key or of `tornect panic`, which comes first and is unchanged, each unattended session it ended past its PAKE suspends its own grant through the host's guard (`Suspended{Panic}`, a `panic_disconnect` line, cleared only by a local Resume): a live session once its `BYE{PANIC}` went out, and a request still being accepted (its block window over, or the read after its reservation done) once its refusal went out, as the Block it stands for. An attended session, a handshake that never passed its PAKE, and an end of one device alone (the guard's own suspend, which ends that device with `PANIC`) suspend nothing; a host without its store serves no unattended session and suspends nothing; the double press stays master off. Should a second session of the same device be ended by the same kill past its PAKE, it writes a second `panic_disconnect` line, and the grant stays `Suspended{Panic}`. `tornect panic` answers "Every handshake and session was ended; a device whose unattended session was ended is suspended until you resume it." | VERIFIED (unit); NEEDS-DEVICE(windows) | `fsm::tests::{a_panic_suspends_the_grant_of_a_live_unattended_session, a_panic_suspends_a_reserved_unattended_session, a_panic_suspends_nothing_for_an_attended_session, a_panic_before_the_pake_suspends_nothing}` (tornect-signal), `host::tests::tornect_panic_suspends_the_grant_behind_an_unattended_session`, `unattended::tests::tornect_panic_says_what_it_did` (apps/tornect); kept: `host::tests::the_panic_key_pressed_twice_within_2_s_turns_unattended_access_off`, `host::tests::ipc_panic_ends_a_live_session`. Device: during an unattended session from the Windows PC, press the panic key once: the session ends at once (under 100 ms), `tornect unattended status` shows the grant suspended by the panic key, and a reconnect is refused as suspended until `tornect unattended resume <device>` |
| "End the session there" (§B.8.4.1, the admin command `end_session`) ends the requester's own session: once the answer went out, the host ends the sessions of the device that asked through the same per-device end a revoke uses (its unattended sessions, live or past their reservation), with `BYE{ADMIN_END}`; other devices' sessions go on, and no host-wide kill fires | VERIFIED (unit) | `fsm::tests::end_session_ends_the_requesters_session_with_admin_end` (tornect-signal; failed first on 2026-10-05) |
| Forget's note when the keychain refuses the delete takes the plan's words (§B.8.9.1): "<note> The password remembered for <host> could not be deleted from <keychain>; Rotate ID deletes it later.", the host named by its pinned label in quotes, as the connect's notes name it (its short ID where it has no pin). The window then lists no computer to forget (Forget took the pin and the address-book entry), so it cannot offer Forget again: the note, and the plan's sentence, name Rotate ID, and `hosts.toml` keeps the record with `remember` set for the check when the prompt opens. The window's recent activity no longer repeats that the administrator level was not given, which the Home status line says; its other notes, the status line and the command line's sentence are unchanged | VERIFIED (unit) | `ui::backend::tests::{forget_is_not_offered_again_after_a_refused_delete, the_window_does_not_repeat_the_level_not_given_note}` (apps/tornect; the first, on the code before the change, showed the address book listing no computer to forget after a refused delete and failed only on the note's last words; the second failed first; both on 2026-10-05) |
| The turn-off sentence's addition (§B.8.7.1 ⑤a, `tornect unattended set-level <device> none`) is said only of a device that has control, and ends with a step the command line offers, `tornect unattended disable <device>`: "To stop that now, disable <device>." (the command line has no command that sets a device to view only); a device with view only, which reaches nothing that runs as administrator, gets the turn-off sentence alone, whatever else holds the level | VERIFIED (unit) | `unattended::tests::the_turn_off_addition_is_not_said_for_a_view_only_device` (apps/tornect; failed first on 2026-10-05); `unattended::tests::set_level_prints_the_turn_off_sentence_with_its_condition` with its two changed endings (below) |
| A change of a grant's options that changes nothing, as `tornect unattended set-level <device> none` on a grant without the level (on Linux, every grant), logs no `GrantChanged` line and writes nothing (§B.8.7.4): decided under the store lock, after the store was found loaded, the grant found and the options applied to it, so a missing grant and a store that is not loaded are still refused; the answer stays "no administrator access; unchanged" | VERIFIED (unit) | `unattended::tests::set_options_that_change_nothing_log_and_write_nothing` (tornect-core), `unattended::tests::set_level_none_at_none_logs_and_writes_nothing` (apps/tornect; both failed first on 2026-10-05); kept: `unattended::tests::set_level_none_on_a_grant_at_none_changes_nothing` (apps/tornect) |
| The polkit action `org.tornect.autostart`, which also guards `tornect install` and `tornect uninstall` on Linux, names them in its dialog: description "Install or remove Tornect, or change whether it starts at login", message "Authentication is required to install or remove Tornect, or to change whether it starts automatically when you log in."; the action's id and defaults are unchanged | NEEDS-DEVICE(linux) | no test reads the file (`reauth::tests::every_guard_operation_maps_to_a_registered_action` keeps its five ids). Device: once the operator has installed the file as root (`sudo install -m 0644 packaging/polkit/org.tornect.policy /usr/share/polkit-1/actions/`), `tornect install` at the build VM's console shows the new message in the polkit dialog, and `tornect service install` shows the same |

## The viewer toolbar (M-UI-4 of the redesign; 2026-10-08)

The stream window's slim bar becomes the redesign's floating toolbar (decision M1-70; `docs/PLAN.md` Part C row 8,
§B.2.1, §B.8.8), in `apps/tornect/src/ui/{pages/viewer,stream,keys,app,view,model}.rs`, and then gains the
screenshot (`ui/screenshot.rs`), the host's notice code (`PONG` key 1, §B.8.4.1: `crates/tornect-signal`,
`apps/tornect/src/secure_screen.rs`, both hosts, `ui/backend.rs`), the lines it carries, Ctrl+Alt+Del by the host's
platform and full screen, with two carried corrections; each commit gate-green on its own tree. The Windows code is
type-checked (`cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --target x86_64-pc-windows-gnullvm`); no test
makes a Windows call, and no test opens a window, an event loop or an injection: the egui pass runs headless with its
accessibility tree, and the forwarder and the chord are pure.

Not done, said here so that nobody reads it as done: a view-only session's viewer is told nothing by the host (the
operator's default of 2026-10-07), so during a UAC prompt or the lock screen it sees a still picture with no word; an
older Windows host never sends the code, so towards it the viewer never says the administrator-windows line and keeps
Ctrl+Alt+Del greyed without a note. [Corrected 2026-10-22, audit B01: until `56c3ef79` no viewer saw a still picture
there, because the GDI capture took a secure screen for a failure and ended the session at once (`CAPTURE_FAILED`);
since then it waits the screen out for up to a minute, and the lock screen ends the session after that minute.
NEEDS-DEVICE(windows), section *Audit fixes (2026-10-22)*.]

The gate is green at **1244 passed, 0 failed, 8 ignored** with this section's last commit (2026-10-08), each
commit of the toolbar gate-green on its own tree before it was committed.

## Settings, sessions and the grant wizard (M-UI-5 of the redesign; 2026-10-08)

The Settings page replaces its placeholder (`docs/PLAN.md` Part C row 10, as the operator ruled on 2026-10-07; decision
M1-71) with the sections that need no operation of §B.8.6.2: General, Connection, Display and quality, Privacy and
About, in `apps/tornect/src/ui/pages/settings/`, a settings change saved by the tokio side (`ui/backend.rs`), the
redacted diagnostics report (`apps/tornect/src/diagnostics.rs`), the viewer opening from its saved defaults
(`ui/pages/viewer.rs`) and the first run's question (`ui/pages/home.rs`, `ui/backend.rs`); each commit gate-green on
its own tree. The Windows code is type-checked only; no test opens a window, starts Tor or writes outside a temporary
home.

| Check | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|

Not done, said here so that nobody reads it as done: the pause's end at 08:00 is OPEN until a zone
source exists. (Corrected 2026-10-24: `config.toml` is replaced atomically since B14,
`887b3691`, in the audit section below.)

## Audit fixes (2026-10-22)

| Finding | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| B01 Windows GDI capture took the secure desktop (a UAC prompt, Ctrl+Alt+Del, the lock screen) for a fatal error, so the whole session ended | VERIFIED (unit) 2026-10-22 for the wait-out; NEEDS-DEVICE(windows) on a real host; the end at a lock OPEN 2026-10-24 (the next row) | `56c3ef79`. A failed grab waits the caller's timeout and answers no new picture; a picture ends the spell; only a minute of failures in a row ends capture, with the grab's error. Tests: `outage::tests::{a_failed_grab_waits_and_says_no_new_picture, only_a_spell_that_lasts_the_limit_ends_capture, the_default_limit_is_a_minute}` (tornect-capture, the logic factored out of `gdi.rs`), `tests::a_source_with_no_picture_for_a_while_keeps_the_session` (tornect-session); `cargo clippy --target x86_64-pc-windows-gnullvm -p tornect-capture -p tornect-session --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean. Recipe: on Windows 10 20H1+ and 11, `tornect.exe host`, a Control session from Linux. (1) From the viewer start an elevated program (`powershell Start-Process cmd -Verb RunAs`): the session stays up, the picture stands still, within about 10 s the viewer says "The computer is showing a secure Windows screen; the person there must answer it"; answer Yes at the host within a minute: the picture moves again and the viewer toasts "The secure Windows screen closed"; the host's log has one "the screen cannot be read" warning and one "the screen can be read again" line. (2) The same with Ctrl+Alt+Del at the host, then Cancel. (3) Leave a prompt up past 60 s: the session ends "screen capture failed" about a minute after it appeared. (4) Win+L: the session ends about a minute later (`BYE{HOST_LOCKED}` is still the plan's, §B.8.8); unlocked within the minute, the picture comes back. (5) Throughout, the host process does not hold a core (Task Manager). Note whether `BitBlt` fails during the prompt or copies a black picture (either keeps the session) |
| B01 review: a Windows host that locks ends its sessions about a minute late, not at once with `BYE{HOST_LOCKED}` (§B.8.6.4; the §B.8.8 row "Windows, locked") | OPEN 2026-10-24 | Before `56c3ef79` the lock screen ended a session at once, by accident (`CAPTURE_FAILED` at the first failed grab). The wait-out cannot tell the lock screen from a UAC prompt or the Ctrl+Alt+Del screen (all three are the Winlogon desktop to `OpenInputDesktop`), so a locked host keeps a session, an unattended one included, with its picture standing still for up to `OUTAGE_LIMIT` (60 s); unlocked within that minute, the session goes on, input included (none reaches the secure desktop meanwhile: UIPI). A GDI failure with no secure desktop also stands still for that minute before capture ends. No interface promises that a lock ends a session; the B01 recipe's step (4) says what happens today. To do: the plan's lock watch (`WTSRegisterSessionNotification`'s `WTS_SESSION_LOCK`, or the `SessionFlags` of `WTSQuerySessionInformationW(WTSSessionInfoEx)`) ends every session `BYE{HOST_LOCKED}` at once, the wait-out kept for UAC and Ctrl+Alt+Del; whether an attended session ends at the lock too is to be decided with it (the plan's sentence is the unattended one). Recipe (Windows 10 20H1+ and 11): `tornect.exe host`, an unattended session from Linux, Win+L at the host: today the session ends about a minute later, "screen capture failed"; with the watch, at once, the viewer told the host locked |
| B06 A control session whose host has no injector still said Control; the viewer's clicks were dropped with no word | VERIFIED (unit) 2026-10-22; NEEDS-DEVICE(linux-desktop) on a GNOME host | `489aac55`. Notice bit `0x08` (`messages::notice::NO_INPUT`) in the host's `PONG`, added by `MediaHost::viewer_notice` to both hosts' codes; the window's line under the toolbar and in the details. Tests: `tests::{a_control_session_with_no_injector_tells_its_viewer, the_viewer_of_a_control_session_with_no_injector_hears_it_at_its_next_ping}` (tornect-session, the second through the real handshake), `ui::pages::viewer::tests::{the_no_input_line_is_said_only_when_the_host_says_so, a_view_only_session_shows_no_notice_whatever_the_host_sends}` (app). Recipe: on a GNOME Wayland host with no stored grant (delete `portal-restore-token`), ask for a Control session from another computer, Allow in Tornect, then Share in GNOME's dialog with "Allow Remote Interaction" off: within about 10 s the viewer's toolbar comes back with "The remote computer did not allow control: you can watch, but your mouse and keyboard reach nothing there.", also under the details' Mode; the host says its viewer's input reaches nothing; a view-only session, and a Control session with the switch on, say nothing of it |
| B07 `encoded_size` clamped each axis to 3840x2160, cropping portrait screens the encoder could send whole | VERIFIED (unit) 2026-10-22; a real upright screen NEEDS-DEVICE(windows) | `6f5ae3ea`. At most 3840x2160 wide, at most 2160x3840 upright. Tests: `tests::{the_crop_is_clamped_in_the_pictures_own_orientation, a_portrait_picture_is_encoded_whole}` (tornect-codec; the second encodes 1200x2400 and decodes it whole, and encodes 2160x3840), `tests::a_position_over_the_visible_region_lands_inside_it` (tornect-input), `wayland::tests::the_pointer_spans_the_encoded_region_edge_to_edge` (tornect-capture). Recipe: a Windows host whose only screen is a 1440x2560 portrait monitor (or a rotated 4K one): the viewer's badge says 1440×2560 (2160×3840), the taskbar shows at the bottom of the picture, and a click on Start opens it |
| B15 `BitBlt` with `CAPTUREBLT` made the host's own pointer flicker during every Windows session | NEEDS-DEVICE(windows) | `3edaa1dd`: the grab is a plain `SRCCOPY` (`RASTER_OP`). Test: `gdi::tests::the_grab_copies_without_captureblt` (Windows only: compiled by the gnullvm clippy, run on Windows). Recipe: on Windows 10 and 11, `tornect.exe host`, a session from Linux; move the pointer on the host for 30 s: it does not flicker; a tooltip, a context menu with a shadow and a taskbar flyout still show in the viewer's picture |
| B16 A Wayland input handle left in the backend kept the last full-size picture of the screen after a session that never started | VERIFIED (unit) 2026-10-22 for the picture slot; the backend's handle by review; NEEDS-DEVICE(linux-desktop) for the memory | `979aedf5`: `Shared::close` (called first by `PwCapture::drop`) drops the unread picture and the spare buffer and takes no other; `SystemBackend::session_ended` drops the kept handle; an open that finishes after its session ended keeps none. Test: `wayland::pipewire::tests::a_closed_slot_keeps_no_picture` (tornect-capture). Recipe: on a GNOME Wayland host at 4K, ask for a Control session and leave (Ctrl-C the viewer) while GNOME's dialog is up, then press Share: the host's resident memory (`ps -o rss`) is back to where it was before the session, not about 33 MB above it |
| B02 A Tor bootstrap, publish or listener failure was terminal: the window stayed offline until Tornect was restarted | VERIFIED (unit) 2026-10-24 for the retry timer (`Restarting<T>`), the countdown, the model and the pages; the run loop's wiring by review only; review fixes VERIFIED (unit) 2026-10-24; a real outage NEEDS-DEVICE(linux-desktop) | `afa549c5`. After a failure (an error or a panic) the next try waits 30 s, doubling to at most 5 min, reset by a start that works (`Restarting<T>`; since `2011f4c9`, one that stayed up 5 min, below); Tor's start and the publishing of this ID each have one, and a listener that closes counts as a failed publish; each Tor try builds its ladder from the start's configuration, so decision M1-40 holds for every try. Home and Settings, Connection say what to check in plain words (no command, file or setting key), count down to the next try and offer "Try again now" (`Command::RetryTor`, which does nothing while a start runs). Tests: `ui::backend::tests::{the_retry_wait_doubles_up_to_five_minutes, a_failed_start_is_tried_again_after_its_wait, try_again_ends_the_wait_and_only_the_wait, the_model_through_a_failure_and_its_try, a_tor_failure_is_said_in_plain_words}`, `ui::pages::tests::a_tor_failure_counts_down_to_its_next_try_and_offers_one_now`. Corrected 2026-10-24 (review): no test drives `run`'s select loop (a due try calling `spawn_tor` again, a closed listener arming the publish, Try again now reaching Tor or the publish): the loop holds a real `TorNode` and `OnionListener`, which no test builds without Tor, so that wiring is checked by review, and by the recipe below. Review fixes `2011f4c9`: (1) a publish that returned reset the count, so an onion service that stops at once after each publish was published again every 30 s for good; a start's failure is the first in a row again only once it stayed up `STAYED_UP` (5 min), so such a service backs off to 5 min; (2) Settings, Connection drew "Try again now" for a publish's try under "Tor: connected"; it shows it for Tor's try only (Home keeps it beside the hosting line). Tests: `ui::backend::tests::{a_publish_that_stops_at_once_keeps_backing_off, a_failed_start_is_tried_again_after_its_wait}`, `ui::pages::tests::a_tor_failure_counts_down_to_its_next_try_and_offers_one_now` (extended). Not covered: the headless `tornect host` still exits when Tor does not start, and the older `tornect-host.service` unit restarts it only after a crash (`Restart=on-abnormal`; `afa549c5`'s message says systemd's `Restart=` applies, which is wrong for this unit, as `2011f4c9`'s says); the sign-in start (XDG autostart on Linux, the at-logon task on Windows) runs the window, `--background`. Recipe: on a GNOME desktop with the network off (`nmcli networking off`), start `tornect --background` hosting: once every way into Tor has failed (at most about 6 minutes) Home says "Tor: not reachable. Check that this computer is online. … Trying again in 30 s." with "Try again now", and incoming requests are off; turn the network on (`nmcli networking on`): at the next try (at most 5 min later), or at once with Try again now, Tor is ready, the hosting line goes to publishing and then reachable, and `tornect ping <id>` from another device answers, without restarting Tornect |
| B10 Alt+F4 in the viewer's stream window ended the session instead of reaching the remote computer | VERIFIED (unit) 2026-10-24; review fix VERIFIED (unit) 2026-10-24, a real Mac NEEDS-DEVICE(macos); real keyboards NEEDS-DEVICE(windows) and NEEDS-DEVICE(linux-desktop) | `011688f2`. A close request that comes with Alt held, in a control session whose keyboard is on the remote computer (not taken back with Ctrl+Alt+Shift+M, not paused, the picture in), sends F4 down and up after the Alt already sent and keeps the session; every other close ends it (the title bar's with no Alt held, a view-only or paused session, Alt+F4 after the chord took the keyboard back, decision M1-70), and End session stays one click away in the toolbar; Send keys has Alt+F4. Tests: `ui::app::tests::{alt_f4_in_a_control_session_goes_to_the_remote_computer, every_other_close_request_ends_the_session, send_keys_sends_alt_f4}`; `docs/research/slice.md` §2 says what the window does with the close. Review fix `92b46a32`: on macOS Option+F4 closes nothing and an Option-click on the close button closes every window, so a close there with Option held ends the session as any other (`ALT_CLOSE_IS_ALT_F4`, not macOS); test `ui::app::tests::a_close_with_option_held_on_macos_ends_the_session` (the decision takes the platform as a parameter); recipe: on macOS 13+, a control session, Option-click the stream window's close button: the session ends and no F4 reaches the host. Not done: on Windows and Linux a title-bar close clicked with Alt held still reads as Alt+F4 (the window gets no event for that click); End session in the toolbar ends it. Recipe: from a Windows 11 viewer and from a GNOME Wayland viewer, a control session to a host with Notepad (or a text editor) focused: Alt+F4 in the stream window closes that program on the host and the session stays; type afterwards: plain letters arrive (no Alt or F4 held there); the title bar's close still ends the session; Ctrl+Alt+Shift+M, then Alt+F4: the session ends; Send keys → Alt+F4 closes the host's focused window on both |
| B25 Cancelling a dial, or a dial that ended early, showed every step ticked under "The session ended" | VERIFIED (unit) 2026-10-24; review fix VERIFIED (unit) 2026-10-24 | `c10e1604`. Both cancel paths record `Failure::Cancelled` at the step the dial had reached ("Cancelled", in the plain colours, its step crossed in the muted colour); an unreadable pins file and "paired, but no session came of it" record `Failure::Other`; `Dial::went_live` is set where the session goes live, and an Ended stage with no failure reads "The session ended" with every step done only for a dial that went live, otherwise "Not connected" with no step done or under way. Tests: `ui::backend::tests::a_cancel_is_recorded_at_its_step`, `ui::pages::tests::a_cancelled_dial_is_not_a_session_that_ended`. Review fix `41178cba`: three paths ended a connect before anything was dialled without a dial of their own ("Tor is not connected yet." for Connect and for an unattended connect, "No password: none was typed and none is remembered."), so the stepper showed the previous dial's ticks or failure; they now start a dial of the ID asked for with no step done (`not_dialled`, as the own-ID refusal does), and Try again dials that ID. Test: `ui::backend::tests::a_connect_that_dials_nothing_forgets_the_last_dial` |
| B26 The Connect card accepted this computer's own Tornect ID | VERIFIED (unit) 2026-10-24 | `652b5eb1`. The connect field says "That is this computer's own ID." as Add computer does, Connect stays disabled, Enter asks for nothing, and no look-alike warning or "Connect anyway" is offered for it (the old warning called it "a different address"); the backend refuses Connect, Connect anyway and an unattended connect to its own onion ID before anything is checked or dialled, and the stepper reads "Not connected" with the reason. Tests: `ui::pages::tests::the_connect_field_refuses_this_computers_own_id`, `ui::backend::tests::this_computers_own_id_is_refused` |
| B27 No way to remove (unpair) a paired device that has no unattended grant | VERIFIED (unit) 2026-10-24; review fix VERIFIED (unit) 2026-10-24; a real host NEEDS-DEVICE(linux-desktop) | `6911a668`. Each card of the address book's Devices tab offers "Remove device…", which asks first (the device's pinned label and device words, and what it takes to come back; never its key), then sends the same `ManagerOp::Remove` as Settings, Security's row: the guard's unpair (a grant revoked with its tombstone; without one a no-op), then the pin (decision M1-25: never a grant for an unpaired device). While unattended access's store failed closed it changes nothing and says why; a pin that went is logged `device_unpaired` (`by` ui, the device, its pinned label), a new `SessionEvent`; a live session of the device ends `BYE{REVOKED}`, attended ones included; why a removal was not done is said on the tab too; without the window host's guard no card offers it. Tests: `ui::backend::tests::{remove_device_unpairs_a_device_without_unattended_access, a_pairing_that_cannot_be_written_says_so_on_the_devices_tab, remove_device_changes_nothing_while_the_store_failed_closed}`, `ui::pages::tests::the_devices_tab_removes_a_device_after_asking`, `session_log::tests::the_level_and_the_helper_events_reach_the_log` (the event's name). Not done: the host's command line has no `devices remove` yet (it would need an IPC operation through the guard). Review fix `ff94e7aa`: a removal whose file could not be written forgets the pin in memory (deny-direction) and says so, but a second Remove on the card, which the tab still shows from the file, found no pin and returned quietly; `TrustedDeviceStore::remove` now looks in the file for a pin it no longer holds and writes its removal again (the label comes back with it, for the `device_unpaired` line), so every Remove says the pairing was kept while the file stays unwritable. Tests: `pairing::tests::a_removal_that_was_not_written_is_written_again` (tornect-signal), `ui::backend::tests::a_pairing_that_cannot_be_written_says_so_on_the_devices_tab` (a second Remove). Recipe: pair a second device with this computer (no unattended access), then Address book → Devices that can connect here → Remove device… on its card: the question names it with its device words; Keep it: nothing changes; Remove device… → Remove: the card goes and `sessions.log` has one `device_unpaired` line with its label; that device's next connect is told the pairing was lost, and after it forgets this computer, connecting compares a code again. Repeat for a device with unattended access: its grant goes from Settings, Security as well |
| B03 Unattended pre-emption answered the reconnecting request `NOT_GRANTED` instead of letting it in, and the A7 test never checked the new request | VERIFIED (unit) 2026-10-24; review fix VERIFIED (unit) 2026-10-24 | `ab16c6c9`. The pre-empting stream waits, at most 5 s (`PREEMPT_SETTLE`), for the cancelled handshake to settle its charge before it charges its own, and past the bound is answered the retryable `UA_BUSY`; a charge refusal that is not the gate's (a charge still pending, a store write that failed) is `UA_BUSY`, never `NOT_GRANTED`; the cancel is level-triggered (a flag plus `Notify`, the waiter enabled before the flag is read), so a pre-emption between `mark_charged` and the first poll of the `AwaitKe3` race on another worker thread is not lost; a handler dropped while charged and unsettled settles its charge as `disconnected` (it used to stay pending and refuse the device until the next restart). Tests (tornect-signal): `unattended::tests::{a_newer_request_preempts_a_stale_charged_handshake, a_preemption_across_worker_threads_lets_the_newer_request_in, a_preemption_before_the_stale_handshake_waits_still_cancels_it, a_charge_still_pending_is_answered_busy_never_not_granted, a_handshake_dropped_while_charged_settles_its_charge}`, each seen failing against a mutant of its fix (the wait removed, the waiter not enabled, the busy answer turned back into `NOT_GRANTED`, the drop's settle removed). The A7 row above is corrected. Not decided here (a policy question, left as the plan has it): whether a ladder lock armed by the stale charge should stop the pre-empting request at the gate. Review fix `5d7f0b1c`: the wait put up to 5 s between the gate and the charge, and a `debug_assert` that the gate's grant is the charged one panicked the handler (debug builds, tests) when the owner revoked and granted the device again meanwhile; the gate's grant is no longer kept, and what must hold, that the PAKE runs on the charged grant's record (read outside the limiter's lock just before the charge), is checked at run time: on a mismatch the charge is settled and the request answered `UA_BUSY`. Test: `unattended::tests::a_grant_renewed_while_a_preemption_settles_is_the_one_used` (panicked on the assertion) |
| B04 The host never noticed a requester leaving while a pairing or session card was open: an Allow then pinned a device that had gone or accepted into a dead stream, and the stale card answered the device's next request `BUSY` / `IN_FLIGHT` for up to 60 s | VERIFIED (unit, the real FSM over a pipe) 2026-10-24; review test VERIFIED (unit) 2026-10-24; the window's card going NEEDS-DEVICE(linux-desktop) | `e1a03a9f`. `Channel::departure` resolves when the peer closes or breaks the stream while the host decides (bytes it sends meanwhile, up to one frame, reach the next read whole); `serve_host` races the pairing card, the session card and the administrator helper's start against it, and the unattended hook races the ask-before-start popup and the block window against `AuthExchange::departed`. A requester that left: its card is dropped (the window's `OnScreen` guard takes it down), nothing is pinned or started, no temp-ban reject is counted, its in-flight slot is free, and `sessions.log` gets a `pair_rejected` or `session_denied` line with the detail `REQUESTER_LEFT` (`AuditEvent::RequesterLeft`). Tests (tornect-signal): `fsm::tests::{a_requester_that_leaves_takes_its_pairing_card_down, a_requester_that_leaves_takes_its_session_card_down, an_unattended_requester_that_leaves_takes_the_block_window_down, bytes_the_requester_sends_during_the_card_reach_the_next_read, a_requester_that_left_is_logged_as_a_refusal}`, `unattended::tests::a_requester_that_leaves_takes_the_block_window_and_the_popup_down`; the card tests and the end-to-end one fail with the watch disabled. Recipe: host in the window on a GNOME desktop; from a second device press Connect, then Cancel while the host's card is up: the card leaves the host's screen within a second or two (Tor's END), `sessions.log` has a `session_denied` line with the detail `REQUESTER_LEFT` (the Sessions page lists it as refused), and pressing Connect again at once opens a new card (not "busy"); the same for a first pairing (Cancel during the code card: the card goes, a `pair_rejected` line with `REQUESTER_LEFT` is logged, and the device is not in Devices that can connect here) and for an unattended connect to a grant with a block window (Ctrl-C the client during the window: the window closes and no sharing indicator appears). Review: on the terminal host a question withdrawn so (the requester's Cancel, at a moment it chooses) is the B22 mechanism, which `a26bbeb5` covers (the closed question says so before the next shows; a line within a second of a question is ignored); `6f61f03a` adds the test of a question withdrawn through `CliConsent` with another device's queued behind it: `prompt::tests::a_question_withdrawn_by_its_requester_never_takes_the_next_answer` (app) |
| B11 Pairing pinned the device before `PAIR_ACCEPT` was written, and the log line of every host decision was skipped when its answer could not be written | VERIFIED (unit, the real FSM over a stream whose writes break) 2026-10-24; review fix VERIFIED (unit) 2026-10-24 | `0b8c3896`. `PAIR_ACCEPT` is written before the device is pinned: a write that fails pins nothing and is logged as the requester leaving (`pair_rejected`, `REQUESTER_LEFT`); a written accept still proves no delivery, and that half-paired state stays decision M1-55's. Every refusal (pairing: banned, in flight, rate limited, busy, timeout, denied; the attended session's; the unattended reject) is logged first, then answered; a `SESSION_ACCEPT` that cannot be written starts nothing and is logged as the requester leaving. Tests (tornect-signal): `fsm::tests::{a_pairing_accept_that_cannot_be_written_pins_nothing_and_is_logged, a_refusal_that_cannot_be_written_is_still_logged, a_session_accept_that_cannot_be_written_starts_nothing_and_is_logged}`, each failing on the previous `host.rs`. Corrected 2026-10-24 (review): the reorder made a pin whose file write fails after the accept went out leave no line at all (the store's error returned before `PairAccepted`), while `TrustedDeviceStore::pin` had changed its in-memory copy, so the device was trusted until the host restarted. Review fix `daad198d`: the store rolls its in-memory copy back when the write fails, and the FSM logs `AuditEvent::PairNotSaved` as a `pair_rejected` line with the detail `PAIR_NOT_SAVED`; the requester was told it is paired, the host's file, memory and log say it is not (decision M1-55's half-paired state). Tests (tornect-signal): `fsm::tests::{a_pairing_that_cannot_be_saved_is_logged_and_not_trusted, a_pairing_not_saved_is_logged_as_a_refusal}`, `pairing::tests::a_pin_that_was_not_written_is_not_kept` |
| B05 Non-ASCII text in an ID field panicked `TornectId::from_str`, which closed the window while a name like "Zoë's laptop" was typed into Connect or Add computer | VERIFIED (unit) 2026-10-24 | `8b45905f`, seeds `1615fe8b`. The `.onion` strip takes the tail with `str::get`, which answers `None` off a character boundary instead of panicking; every input that parsed before parses the same. Tests (tornect-core): `id::tests::{non_ascii_text_is_an_error_not_a_panic (every typed prefix of three names, for TornectId and DeviceId), from_str_never_panics (proptest, any String), from_str_never_panics_near_the_suffix (proptest, non-ASCII next to a real body and the suffix)}`, all three failing on the old slice (panic at `id.rs`), their shrunk cases kept in `proptest-regressions/id.txt` |
| B12 The reconnect cooldown was never enforced: `settle(Ok)` zeroed `last_used`'s length before `post_f2` read it, and nothing recorded a session's end | VERIFIED (unit, and the real host over a pipe) 2026-10-24; review fix VERIFIED (unit) 2026-10-24 | `4f166fa3`: `settle(Ok)` leaves `last_used` while the cooldown of the session it records runs, so `post_f2` answers `COOLDOWN`; `GrantStore::record_session_start`. `29fea995`: the `COOLDOWN` reject carries `retry_after_s` (§B.8.4.6), which it never did. `aa6bd4cc`: both hosts serve sessions through `host::UnattendedUse`, which records each unattended session's length on its grant when it ends, counted from the PAKE success in `last_used.at` (so `at + duration_secs` is the end), only for a device's latest session. Tests: `unattended::tests::b12_a_full_length_session_is_followed_by_the_reconnect_cooldown` (tornect-core; charge, settle(Ok), start, a 4 h end, then COOLDOWN{600}, COOLDOWN{300}, a session once it ran out, none after 1 h; fails on the old `settle`), `unattended::tests::the_cooldown_reject_carries_the_time_left` (tornect-signal), `host::tests::{a_session_that_ran_its_maximum_is_followed_by_the_cooldown (a real unattended session through `wire()` past a 1 s maximum, then the right password refused `COOLDOWN` with 590-600 s; fails with the sink taken out), the_use_sink_records_the_latest_unattended_session_only}` (tornect). Review fix `bd787630`: `aa6bd4cc` rounded both parts of the length down (the lead from `at`, the monotonic time the session ran), so a session cut at its signed `ends_at` nearly always read `max_session_secs - 1` and left no cooldown; the length is now the wall clock's span in the whole seconds `at` and `ends_at` count in (a session cut at `ends_at` ends exactly there), or the monotonic span rounded down where that is longer (the wall went back during the session). Test: `host::tests::a_session_cut_at_its_signed_end_is_followed_by_the_cooldown` (tornect; five sub-second phases of the PAKE, the start and the cut, each followed by `COOLDOWN` with exactly 600 s left, and a session during which the wall went back an hour; fails with the old length, 14399 s of 14400). Corrected 2026-10-24 (review): what is not covered. The cooldown follows a session whose length, so counted from the PAKE success (`last_used.at`), reached `max_session_secs`, however it ended; no host cuts a session at its signed `ends_at` yet (the open M-FEAT-3 F-4 item). A session whose host process ends abruptly (killed, crashed, the machine switched off) records no end, so no cooldown follows it, even after a full-length session. A wall clock that jumps forward during a session counts it longer, which may give a cooldown to a shorter one |
| B13 Store maintenance never ran: no `sessions.log` verifier at start-up, no `GrantExpired` line or 30-day pruning, no recovery load after a locked keystore | VERIFIED (unit, the real host wiring) 2026-10-24; review fixes VERIFIED (unit) 2026-10-24; the row on a real desktop NEEDS-DEVICE(linux-desktop) | `def1366d`: the verifier reads raw lines, so a line cut inside a multi-byte character is a torn line, not an I/O error that would fail every start; the readiness row `session_log` ("Session log intact", badge only, "log modified or truncated at line N of <file>: <reason>" for a broken chain). `2f3c1438`: `wire_window` (terminal and window host) runs the verifier, then the housekeeping, before serving; the 10-minute re-probe runs the housekeeping and, while the store is failed closed on its keystore, the one recovery load followed by the verifier. Tests: `session_log::tests::a_line_cut_inside_a_character_is_a_torn_line` and `unattended::readiness::tests::the_session_log_row_says_where_the_log_broke` (tornect-core; the first fails on the old reader with `InvalidData`), `host::tests::{a_tampered_session_log_is_reported_at_the_start, the_start_logs_expired_grants_and_prunes_them, the_upkeep_loads_a_store_whose_keystore_was_locked}` (tornect; the first two fail with the start-up upkeep taken out). Recipe: `TORNECT_HOME=$PWD/target/tornect-test/h1 cargo run -p tornect`, turn unattended access on and give a paired device a grant, close the window; change one digit of the time in the second line of `target/tornect-test/h1/logs/sessions.log`; start the window again: stderr says "warning: sessions.log: log modified or truncated at line N of sessions.log: …", N being the edited line or the next one (whose link it breaks), and Settings, Security's readiness list reads "Session log intact: FAIL: log modified or truncated at line N …"; undo the edit and restart: "Session log intact: ok". Not built: a banner on Home or in the tray for it (the row and the not-ready badge are what shows it). Review fixes, 2026-10-24: (1) `f3c220ed`: this device's own `UnattendedConnect` lines (`tornect connect --unattended`, the window's unattended connects and admin commands) were written untagged through a handle without `K_log`, which the verifier, once wired, read as "untagged line after the chain started" at every start of a host with unattended access on; the window now writes them through its host role's store (tagged, the head kept, one writer in the process) and a process without a host keys its own handle with the home's `K_log` (`GrantStore::current_log_key`; untagged only where the host writes untagged lines, nothing written while `K_master` exists but cannot be read). Tests: `host::tests::this_devices_connect_lines_keep_the_log_intact` (fails with the old writer), `ua_client::tests::a_line_without_a_host_is_keyed_as_the_store_keys_its_own`, `ui::backend::tests::the_window_logs_its_connects_through_its_host_role` (tornect). (2) `08706ad0`: a store failed closed for a reason other than its keystore still installs `K_log` but gives no head or key epoch, so its log was checked against the current key from the first line, and a log spanning a *Rotate ID* read "tag does not verify"; a store that is not loaded now has its log's links checked only (`SessionLog::verify_links`), and the row warns "the unattended store could not be read, so only the log's links were checked, not its tags or its end". Test: `unattended::tests::b13_a_store_that_failed_closed_checks_the_links_of_a_log_across_key_epochs` (tornect-core; fails with the tags checked). (3) `98fe8658`: the load itself can append lines before the start's verifier runs (an abandoned charge settled, a machine-id change, a clock anomaly, lines held while the keystore was locked), each linking to the file's tail, so a tail cut while no host ran read as a whole chain; a host load now verifies the log before its first append and the start reports a break found there (`GrantStore::take_found_log`). Tests: `unattended::tests::b13_the_host_load_judges_the_log_before_its_own_lines` (tornect-core), `host::tests::a_cut_tail_is_reported_although_the_load_appends_first` (tornect; read Ok before). Not changed: two processes appending to one home's log at the same moment (a window host and a terminal's `tornect connect`) can still fork the chain, since no lock spans processes; a live log whose tail is cut while the host runs is healed by the next append (the verifier then finds a whole chain) |
| B14 `Config::save` rewrote `config.toml` in place, so a crash mid-save could leave it empty (every setting, bridges included, back to its default) or unreadable | VERIFIED (unit) 2026-10-24 | `887b3691`: `keystore::write_private_file_atomic` (`<name>.tmp` written `0600` and synced, renamed over the file, the directory synced on Unix), used by `Config::save`. Test (tornect-core): `config::tests::a_save_replaces_the_file_whole_or_not_at_all` (a save whose temporary file cannot be written leaves `config.toml` byte-identical and loadable, which the in-place write fails; a save leaves no temporary file, mode 0600 and a new inode). Not changed: the window still refuses to start on an unreadable `config.toml`; moving it aside and starting with defaults would turn off settings such as paranoid mode without the user's say, which is left for a product decision |
| B08 The Windows panic-key hook kept Ctrl and Alt held after Ctrl+Alt+Del, so a later Ctrl+End, or a bare End, pressed the panic key (pressed twice: unattended access off, persisted) | VERIFIED (unit) 2026-10-24 for the modifier logic; both hooks NEEDS-DEVICE(windows) | `1bf717d7`, `981a7f4d`. A low-level hook is not called while the secure desktop takes the input, so the key-ups of Ctrl+Alt+Del (and of keys let go during a UAC prompt or on the lock screen) never reached it. As the chord's key goes down, the host's hook and the administrator helper's chord hook now read the system's key state (`GetAsyncKeyState`): a modifier the hook saw go down counts only while the system still has it down, and one it has up is forgotten. Injected events still never make a modifier count; a modifier with an injected event since its last physical one keeps what the hook saw until its next physical event, so a remote viewer's injected key-ups cannot hold the panic key off. That rule also keeps a stale modifier counted while the viewer's injected events of it keep the system's state out of the question: after Ctrl+Alt+Del, a Control-session viewer's Ctrl+C and Alt+Tab made the owner's bare End the chord again (Ctrl+End needed only Alt injected), found in review. So each hook's thread also sets an out-of-context WinEvent hook for `EVENT_SYSTEM_DESKTOPSWITCH`, and every switch of the input desktop forgets all the hook saw (`Modifiers::desktop_switched`); a chord pressed afresh after the switch works at once. The logic is `hotkey::Modifiers`, shared by both hooks (one thread-local, `hotkey::win::MODS`). Not covered: a modifier whose key-up the hook misses without a desktop switch (for example, should the host's medium-integrity hook miss keys while an elevated window has focus, UNVERIFIED in §B.8.7.5) still stays counted after the viewer injects that modifier, until its next physical event; and should `EVENT_SYSTEM_DESKTOPSWITCH` not reach the hook across the secure desktop, the Ctrl+Alt+Del case above is open again (recipe step 7 tells). Tests (tornect-platform): `hotkey::tests::{a_modifier_let_go_on_the_secure_desktop_does_not_linger, injected_modifiers_neither_press_nor_hold_off_the_panic_key, a_desktop_switch_forgets_modifiers_injected_input_kept_stale, the_chord_names_its_modifier_bits}` (the first two fail when the system's state is ignored, the third when a desktop switch forgets nothing); `cargo clippy --target x86_64-pc-windows-gnullvm -p tornect-platform --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean. Recipe (adds to TM-61): on Windows 10 20H1+ and 11, a host with unattended access on and a live session from another device. (1) Ctrl+Alt+Del, then Cancel; in Notepad press End twice and Ctrl+End twice: the session stays and `tornect unattended status` still says on. (2) Ctrl+Alt+End ends the session at once. (3) Open a UAC prompt (Ctrl+Shift+Enter on a program in Start), answer it, then Ctrl+End twice: nothing; Ctrl+Alt+End ends a session. (4) Win+L, sign in again, then End and Ctrl+End: nothing. (5) In a Control session the owner holds Ctrl+Alt on the host's keyboard while the viewer presses and lets go of Ctrl and Alt on theirs; the owner then presses End: the session ends. (6) With an elevated window focused during an administrator session (the helper's hook), repeat (1) and (2): the same. (7) In a Control session: Ctrl+Alt+Del on the host, then Cancel; the viewer presses Ctrl+C, then Alt+Tab, on theirs; the owner presses End twice in Notepad, then Ctrl+End twice: the session stays and `tornect unattended status` still says on; Ctrl+Alt+End then ends the session. (8) Repeat (7) with Win+L and signing in again instead of Ctrl+Alt+Del: the same |
| B09 XDG start at login could not be turned on, or removed, on Linux distributions without systemd | VERIFIED (unit) 2026-10-24; a real distribution without systemd NEEDS-DEVICE(linux-desktop) | `4b51cf25`. With no `systemctl` where the unit backend looks (`/usr/bin`, `/bin`; no version looked anywhere else), `SystemdUserUnit::no_mechanism` is now true, which the XDG entry takes for a definite "no older unit": the entry installs, reads back and is removed, and nothing is said of an older start at login. A `systemctl` whose user manager does not answer is still never taken for absent, and the executable's location rule is unchanged. Test (tornect-platform): `autostart::tests::without_systemd_the_entry_is_installed_and_removed` (fails with `no_mechanism` false); the unchecked-unit tests `install_and_uninstall_stop_at_an_old_unit_that_cannot_be_checked`, `an_old_unit_that_cannot_be_checked_is_never_reported_absent` are unchanged and green. Recipe: on Void, Alpine or Devuan with an XDG desktop (Xfce or KDE) and polkit, from the release: `tornect service install` (the password is asked) says the start at login is registered and `~/.config/autostart/tornect.desktop` exists; `tornect service status` names no older start at login; sign out and in: Tornect starts in the background; `tornect service uninstall` removes the entry; `tornect install` then `tornect uninstall` both finish without mentioning `systemctl` |
| B17 One accept or pipe-instance error ended the host's control channel (`tornect panic`, the second launch's Show, every `tornect unattended` command) until the host restarted | VERIFIED (unit) 2026-10-24 for the Unix endpoint and the retry; the Windows loop NEEDS-DEVICE(windows) | `815aab9b`, `d08ac50f`. Unix: a failed `accept` pauses the loop (50 ms, doubling to 1 s, reset by an accepted connection) and accepts again; only `EBADF`, `EINVAL` or `ENOTSOCK` (the listening socket itself unusable) end it. Windows: the next instance is made with the same back-off while the connected instance is held, and an instance that cannot be freed, after a refusal or after a client that left before it was served (`d08ac50f`: that path used to ignore the failed `DisconnectNamedPipe` and wait on the same instance every 50 ms, serving nothing and saying nothing), is replaced the same way (`freed_or_replaced`), dropped only once its replacement is made, so the pipe's name never lapses; `FILE_FLAG_FIRST_PIPE_INSTANCE`, the DACL, the account checks and `MAX_CONNECTIONS` are unchanged. Tests (tornect-platform): `ipc::tests::{an_accept_that_fails_does_not_end_the_endpoint (EMFILE, ENFILE, ECONNABORTED, EINTR, then two requests answered; fails when every accept error ends the loop), an_unusable_listener_ends_the_endpoint, only_an_unusable_listener_ends_the_endpoint}`, `ipc::pipe_rules::{a_failure_the_endpoint_outlives_pauses_it_briefly, a_pipe_instance_that_cannot_be_made_yet_is_tried_again, a_pipe_instance_that_cannot_be_freed_is_replaced}` (the last fails when an instance that could not be freed is kept, or dropped before its replacement is made). Recipe (Windows 10 and 11): start `tornect.exe host`; in PowerShell 7 run `1..32 \| ForEach-Object -Parallel { tornect unattended status }` three times; then `tornect panic` and `tornect unattended status` both answer, and the host's output never says the control channel stopped (a "making the IPC pipe's next instance failed; trying again" warning in its log is allowed) |
| B18 The Windows re-authentication dialog had no time limit while the command line gives up after 150 s, so an operation was applied after the command line had reported it failed | VERIFIED (unit) 2026-10-24 for the bound and the late answer; the real CredUI dialog NEEDS-DEVICE(windows) | `8902a5ad`. The dialog and the `LogonUserW` check run on a thread of their own and the operation waits at most `WindowsReAuth::DIALOG_TIMEOUT` (120 s, as polkit's), then is refused (`Denied`, logged `timed_out`); a credential typed later is wiped without being checked; the one-dialog lock is held by the dialog's thread until the dialog returns (CredUI cannot close it), so a second operation meanwhile is refused at once. The compile-time check that `ipc::RESPONSE_TIMEOUT` (150 s) outlasts the dialog by 20 s now covers Windows, on every OS. Test (tornect-platform): `reauth::tests::a_dialog_left_open_past_its_timeout_refuses_and_its_late_password_is_not_checked` (fails with the wait unbounded, and with the late credential checked); `a_second_windows_dialog_is_refused_while_one_is_open` and `the_windows_flow_allows_only_the_current_user` unchanged and green. Recipe: on Windows, `tornect.exe host` with a suspended grant (`tornect unattended suspend <device>`); run `tornect unattended resume <device>` and leave the secure prompt open: after about 120 s, before its own 150 s, the command line reports the re-authentication denied, and the prompt stays on screen; meanwhile a second `resume` says another dialog is already open; now type the right password: `tornect unattended status` still shows the grant suspended and `sessions.log` has no resume line; run `resume` again and answer within the time: it is resumed |

Test: `unattended::tests::a_short_argument_names_a_device_by_its_label_only`; the ambiguity case of the test above now uses a 4-character prefix |
| B20 Two overlapping unattended requests on the terminal host: a typed `block` blocked the earlier one, and the other session started | VERIFIED (unit) 2026-10-24; a real terminal NEEDS-DEVICE(linux-desktop) | `35bc8053`. The lines typed go to the window that opened first, and a line cannot say which request it means, so one `block` now blocks every window open when it is read (fail-safe: a refused session and a suspended grant), each saying "Blocked. <label> …"; a window opened after that `block` is not affected, and a window that opens beside another says "N unattended requests are waiting. Recipe: `tornect host` in a terminal with two devices holding My machines grants (block window 10 s); connect both unattended within a second; the second line says that block blocks both; type `block` once: both clients get `BLOCKED_BY_LOCAL_USER`, no frame, and `tornect unattended status` shows both grants suspended |
| B21 Tornect opened from the menu while `tornect install` asked the sign-in question ran without unattended access, and the install's `y` was refused as "a remote session is live" | VERIFIED (unit) 2026-10-24 for the wording; a real desktop NEEDS-DEVICE(linux-desktop) | `2c233a5d`. [y/N]" waits open Tornect from the menu; answer `y`: the message above, no second dialog, `~/.config/autostart/tornect.desktop` absent; repeat answering Enter: the first sentence; close the window, open it again: Settings, Security works |

Answer again:"; a pairing or session question whose wait is dropped unanswered (its time ran out, or the device went away) says "The request from "<label>" closed without an answer …, so it is refused." before the terminal is let go, so before the next question shows. The stop code uses the same path; a password registered but not printable stops the command with an error that says so; the Windows install's message-box console never holds one. | E3-4 (decisions.md M1-52 (9)) After the signing subkey expires, an `EXPKEYSIG` lyrebird signature was accepted whatever its date | VERIFIED (unit, and gpgv 2.4.4 live on a throwaway key) 2026-10-24 | `cf040bbf`. pt-fetch reads each key's expiry from the pinned key file (`gpg --with-colons --fixed-list-mode --show-keys`) and accepts `EXPKEYSIG` only when `VALIDSIG`'s creation time is before the earlier of the signing subkey's and its primary's expiry (the 15.0.23 subkey: 1793012697, 2026-10-26 11:04:57 UTC); no expiry to compare with, or a time not in seconds, refuses. Live check: a key whose signing subkey expired 2026-10-01 12:00 UTC, a signature of 2026-09-15 and one of 2026-10-15 (made after extending the subkey in another copy of the key): gpgv exited 0 with `EXPKEYSIG` for both; the judge accepted the first and refused the second ("made at 2026-10-15 00:00:00 UTC, after the signing key … expired"). Tests: `pt::tests::{an_expired_key_signature_made_after_the_expiry_is_refused (those status lines), the_key_files_expiry_dates_are_read_per_key, a_good_signature_by_the_pinned_key_is_accepted, any_other_signature_is_refused}`. A real pt-fetch was not run: archive.torproject.org is not reachable from this machine. **Limit (review, 2026-10-24; `1dd6710a`):** the bound reads the signature's own creation time, which its signer chooses, so it refuses only a signature that carries its true date: a holder of the retired subkey can backdate one. Reproduced with gpg/gpgv 2.4.4: a throwaway key whose signing subkey expired 2026-10-01 00:00 UTC (1790856000), a signature made on 2026-10-24 under `--faked-system-time 20260930T000000`: gpgv exited 0 with `EXPKEYSIG` and `VALIDSIG … 2026-09-30 1790726400`, before the expiry, the same lines an honest signature of that date gives, which the judge accepts. The SHA-256 pins still decide what ships; the release notes keep E3-4 in their build-process list with that wording, as do `SOURCES.md` step 3, BUILD-INFO.txt's signature line ("after the time the signature gives") and `CONTINUATION.md` |

Before: E0599 (`no method named start_paused` on the runtime Builder) in `wayland/mod.rs` and `wayland/portal.rs`; after: 36 passed, 1 ignored. The other crates that pause tokio's clock (signal, session, platform, the app) already declared it |
| `SECURITY.md`: how to report a vulnerability privately | OPEN (repository setting) 2026-10-24 | `f64b9b3b`: `SECURITY.md` (GitHub private vulnerability reporting on Mxrider007/Tornect, an issue asking for a private contact as the fallback, the scope, no bug bounty); the README points to it. `gh api repos/Mxrider007/Tornect/private-vulnerability-reporting` answered `{"enabled":false}` on 2026-10-24, so the form is not offered yet: the owner turns it on under Settings, Code security, Private vulnerability reporting. **Corrected 2026-10-24 (review; `21006b70`):** its out-of-scope list excluded every attack that "needs … the owner's own account", which contradicted its own scope (the control channel and the re-authentication before a change exist to stop a same-account actor, PLAN §B.8.2 T9). It now excludes only endpoint compromise outside Tornect's surfaces (rewriting its files, keychain entries or executables, as administrator, root or the owner), names T9's accepted administrator-helper residual, and puts in scope widening access through Tornect's own window, control channel or command line without the OS re-authentication or during a live session, from the owner's account or a Control session included |

## Invitation links (design `invite`, revision 2; 2026-10-24)

| Step 1: how a `tornect://` link reaches Tornect (Desktop Entry `%u`/`NoDisplay`/`MimeType`, the MIME Applications spec, GIO, KService, generic `xdg-open`, snapd and portal `OpenURI`, the HKCU `URL Protocol` keys, `RegDeleteTreeW`, browser escaping, CVE-2018-1000006) | VERIFIED (container probes, upstream source) 2026-10-24; the desktop and browser parts NEEDS-DEVICE (rows below) | `docs/research/links.md`. GIO 2.80.0 in isolated `XDG_*`/`HOME` trees: 20 lookup and launch cases (§2.1), reproducing every container claim of the design and adding: GIO appends `%f`, not the URI, to an `Exec` without a field code; `[Added Associations]` beats `[Removed Associations]` in the same file; keys are case-sensitive; CRLF files parse; a `gnome-mimeapps.list` default wins under GNOME. xdg-utils 1.1.3 and 1.2.1 (from the Ubuntu packages, not installed): `xdg-mime query default` names the entry with a `[Default Applications]` line, with only an `[Added Associations]` line, and with no `mimeapps.list` at all (its last resort scans `MimeType=`); it never reads `[Removed Associations]`. Wine 9.0: the registry calls and dispositions of design §4.5, and `RegDeleteTreeW(HKCU, key)` deleting the key itself (§4.3); a Rust 1.96 argv printer behind `"<exe>" open-link -- "%1"` shows every word a `"` splits off landing after `--` (§4.4). Not present here: `xdg-mime`, `xdg-open`, `update-desktop-database`, `kde-open`, a browser |
| Generic `xdg-open` runs nothing for an `Exec` whose items are quoted (`desktop_exec` quotes every item) | VERIFIED (unit, xdg-utils probe) 2026-10-24 (step 9); a path that needs quotes stays OPEN by design | Step 9 writes the link entry's `Exec` with `desktop_exec_bare` (tornect-platform `autostart`): an item of letters and digits (any script) and `/ . _ - + , : @ =` stays bare; any other item, the empty one included, is quoted exactly as `desktop_exec` quotes it (the Desktop Entry reserved characters, `%`, and the shell's `[ ] { } !`, since generic `xdg-open` runs the value through `eval`); control characters and non-UTF-8 are refused as before. The menu and autostart entries keep `desktop_exec` (the desktop's launcher runs them, not `xdg-open`). Tests (tornect-platform): `autostart::tests::bare_exec_items_are_bare_quoted_or_refused`, `install::linux::tests::the_link_entry_is_the_designed_text` (`Exec=/home/u/.local/bin/tornect [--home /srv/tornect/h1] open-link -- %u`), `install::linux::tests::the_link_entry_exec_quotes_only_what_needs_it`. Probe here with that pinned text in an isolated `HOME`/`XDG_*` tree and a `#!/bin/sh` argv recorder at `<tree>/home/.local/bin/tornect`, xdg-utils 1.1.3 (`xdg-utils_1.1.3-4.1ubuntu3_all.deb`) and 1.2.1 (`xdg-utils_1.2.1-2ubuntu2_all.deb`) extracted with `dpkg-deb -x`, not installed, `DISPLAY=:99 BROWSER=/bin/false`, no `XDG_CURRENT_DESKTOP` (the generic path): `xdg-mime query default x-scheme-handler/tornect` → `tornect-link.desktop` (no `mimeapps.list` yet: its `MimeType=` scan); `xdg-open tornect://<56 chars>` → exit 0, argv `[open-link] [--] [tornect://<56 chars>]`; `xdg-open 'tornect://abc" --home /x'` → exit 0, argv `[open-link] [--] [tornect://abc" --home /x]` (one argument). The same entry with every item quoted: 1.1.3 `xdg-open: 811: : Permission denied` (exit 4), 1.2.1 `xdg-open: no method available for opening …` (exit 3), the program never run. Left by design: a bin folder or `--home` whose path needs quotes (a space, `~`, …) is quoted, so generic `xdg-open` cannot open links on such an install; GIO and KService still do, and pasting the invitation always works |

Tests (tornect-core): `invite::tests::parse_link_accepts_the_grammar` (`tornect://ID`, `tornect:ID`, `TORNECT://ID.ONION/`, mixed case, surrounding ASCII whitespace), `parse_link_refuses_everything_else_with_its_problem` (33 rows, each with its exact `IdProblem`: `?x=1`, `#f`, `user@`, `:443`, `%41`, `/extra`, `///`, a trailing `//`, a doubled `.onion`, an inner space, hyphen or U+00A0, 129 bytes (`LinkExtras`); `http://`, `https://`, no colon, `tornect`, the empty string (`NotALink`); a `0` (`NeverDigit`); a bad checksum, version 2, a non-curve key; 55 and 57 characters, `tornect:` alone (`Short { found: 0 }`); `!`, `é`, U+200B, U+202E and NUL inside (`NotAlphabet`, the character echoed only for `!`); `tornect:x€onion` and `tornect:€.onion` answered, never a panic), `parse_link_bounds_its_input_before_any_work` (128 bytes with padding parse, 129 do not), `find_id_reads_a_typed_id_in_every_written_form` (bare, upper case, `.onion`, groups, hyphens, U+00A0, a line break), `find_id_names_each_problem_of_a_typed_id` (20 rows incl. `x€onion`, `€.onion`, `abcde€`), `find_id_reads_one_link`, `find_id_takes_the_id_out_of_an_invitation_or_a_sentence` (the invitation, with CRLF, "my id <ID>", quotes, brackets, a 7-group window between 8-letter words), `find_id_says_why_a_text_holds_no_id`, `find_id_refuses_a_text_with_two_ids_or_a_broken_link` (visible ID A with link B is `Several`; a `?x=1` or `/x` link token fails a valid invitation), `find_id_removes_invisible_characters_and_never_bidi_controls` (the six format characters are removed with `stripped`; the eleven bidi controls are `NotAlphabet(None)`; `parse_link` never strips), `find_id_bounds_its_input_before_any_work` (4096 bytes read, 4097 `TooLong`), `rewrite_is_for_links_texts_and_stripped_characters`, `the_invitation_is_the_designed_text` (the exact §2.1 text, LF, no final newline), `a_link_is_the_scheme_and_the_lowercase_id`, `the_fixtures_are_what_they_claim`; `id::tests::is_b32_is_the_onion_alphabet`. `cargo test -p tornect-core --lib -- invite id::` → `29 passed`. Pinned consequences of §4.2's order: a short text that fails as a typed ID but holds one as a token is still found (`Text`); with no ID found, the typed reading's problem wins over a broken candidate's ("my id <typo>" is `Long { found: 60 }`, not `Checksum`); a lone link ending in a sentence's full stop is `LinkExtras` (step 4 reads it strictly) while the same link inside a sentence is trimmed and read; groups joined by ` - ` are 13 tokens, neither typed nor a 7-token window, so they give `NoId` |

Every generated input is checked for: an answer, never a panic; a found ID is a usable key; a link that parses is what `find_id` reads too; the byte bounds decide first; messages hold only printable ASCII, space, `–`, `“`, `”`. `cargo test -p tornect-core --lib -- invite` → `26 passed`; once with `PROPTEST_CASES=20000` → `26 passed` in 6.2 s |

| `RegDeleteTreeW` with a subkey name deletes that key itself on real Windows | NEEDS-DEVICE(windows 10/11) | Wine 9.0 does (links.md §4.3). Recipe, in PowerShell as the signed-in user: `$t = Add-Type -PassThru -Name R -Namespace P -MemberDefinition '[DllImport("advapi32.dll", CharSet = CharSet.Unicode)] public static extern int RegDeleteTreeW(IntPtr h, string s);'`; `New-Item -Force 'HKCU:\Software\Classes\tornectprobe\shell\open\command' \| Out-Null`; `$t::RegDeleteTreeW([IntPtr](-2147483647), 'Software\Classes\tornectprobe')` must print `0`; `Test-Path 'HKCU:\Software\Classes\tornectprobe'` then tells: `False` = the key itself is deleted (the design's `RegDeleteKeyW` afterwards answers 2, counted as success), `True` = only its contents went (the `RegDeleteKeyW` does the rest). Record which |
| How Edge, Chrome and Firefox pass a `"` in a clicked link to the handler on Windows | NEEDS-DEVICE(windows 10/11) | Chromium escapes it to `%22` before launching (links.md §3); Firefox is unverified. Recipe: build the links.md §4.4 printer changed to append its argv to `%TEMP%\tornect-argv.txt`; `reg add HKCU\Software\Classes\tornectprobe /ve /d "URL:probe" /f`, `reg add HKCU\Software\Classes\tornectprobe /v "URL Protocol" /d "" /f`, and under `\shell\open\command` the default value `"<printer>" open-link -- "%1"`; open a local page with `<a href='tornectprobe://abc" --home C:\x'>x</a>` in each browser and click through its prompt; read the file (one line per argv item); then `reg delete HKCU\Software\Classes\tornectprobe /f`. Whatever arrives, every injected word must follow `--` |
